# Spectrum analysis and optimal time decay rates of a kinetic-fluid-Poisson system

**arXiv ID**: 2608.02377v1
**Authors**: Junhao Chen, Hailiang Li, Mingying Zhong
**Published**: 2026-08-03
**Categories**: math.AP
**Comments**: 47 pages, no figures
**HTML URL**: https://arxiv.org/html/2608.02377v1

## Abstract

In this paper, we consider the Cauchy problem for the Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck/Navier-Stokes-Poisson (VPFP/NSP) system, which couples the VPFP system with the compressible NSP system through a friction force dependent on the relative velocity and a self-consistent Poisson equation. Motivated by the spectrum analysis for the Vlasov-Poisson-Boltzmann (VPB) system, we introduce a suitable norm to capture the effect of the forcing induced by the Poisson equation and give a detailed spectrum analysis of the linearized system around a global equilibrium. Our results show that the two coupling mechanisms lead to an essentially different spectrum structure of the coupled system from those of the individual VPFP and NSP systems. More precisely, the low-frequency spectrum contains a pair of acoustic branches with the propagation speed $\sqrt{\frac{γ+1}{2}}~(γ\ge1)$ and two diffusive branches, thereby restoring the usual acoustic wave propagation of classical compressible fluids. Moreover, we establish the global existence of the solution to the nonlinear system and obtain the optimal time decay rate $(1+t)^{-\frac34}$, with a faster rate $(1+t)^{-\frac54}$ for the electric field and relative velocity. The present analysis also provides a useful framework for studying related kinetic-fluid models coupled through friction and self-consistent fields.

## Spectrum analysis and optimal time decay rates of a kinetic-fluid-Poisson systemJunhao Chen1, Hailiang Li2, Mingying Zhong1,3

## Abstract

In this paper, we consider the Cauchy problem for the Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck/Navier-Stokes-Poisson (VPFP/NSP) system, which couples the VPFP system with the compressible NSP system through a friction force dependent on the relative velocity and a self-consistent Poisson equation[4,16,12]. Motivated by the spectrum analysis for the Vlasov-Poisson-Boltzmann (VPB) system[20], we introduce a suitable norm to capture the effect of the forcing induced by the Poisson equation and give a detailed spectrum analysis of the linearized system around a global equilibrium. Our results show that the two coupling mechanisms lead to an essentially different spectrum structure of the coupled system from those of the individual VPFP and NSP systems[16,17]. More precisely, the low-frequency spectrum contains a pair of acoustic branches with the propagation speedγ+12​(γ≥1)\sqrt{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}~(\gamma\geq 1)and two diffusive branches, thereby restoring the usual acoustic wave propagation of classical compressible fluids. Moreover, we establish the global existence of the solution to the nonlinear system and obtain the optimal time decay rate(1+t)−34(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}, with a faster rate(1+t)−54(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}for the electric field and relative velocity. The present analysis also provides a useful framework for studying related kinetic-fluid models coupled through friction and self-consistent fields.

Key words. Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck system, compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson system, kinetic-fluid models, spectrum analysis, optimal time decay rates

## 1Introduction

Kinetic-fluid models play an important role in the study of two-phase flows. In two-phase flows, the disperse phase (kinetic) is usually described using a statistical approach, while the dense phase (fluid) using a hydrodynamic approach[4,14]. These models have extensive specific applications, including the description of diesel engines, the dynamics of sprays, pollution settling processes, rain formation, wastewater treatment and so on[2,3,8,33].

The kinetic-fluid-Poisson system can be used to model the temporal evolution of a collisionless plasma with hot electrons and cold positive ions, and capture the kinetic effects such as electron phase space holes, with related applications including the study of the nonlinear electrostatic waves, the generation and propagation of ion‑acoustic solitons[1,12]. In the present paper, we consider the Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck/Navier-Stokes-Poisson (VPFP/NSP) system in three dimension:{∂tF+v⋅∇xF+∇xΦ⋅∇vF=∇v⋅[∇vF+(v−u)​F],∂tϱ+divx​(ϱ​u)=0,∂t(ϱ​u)+divx​(ϱ​u⊗u)−Δx​u+∇p+ϱ​∇xΦ=∫ℝ3(v−u)​F​𝑑v,Δx​Φ=∫ℝ3F​𝑑v−ϱ.\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}F+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F+\nabla_{x}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{v}F=\nabla_{v}\cdot[\nabla_{v}F+(v-u)F],\\
&\partial_{t}\varrho+{\rm div}_{x}(\varrho u)=0,\\
&\partial_{t}(\varrho u)+{\rm div}_{x}(\varrho u\otimes u)-\Delta_{x}u+\nabla p+\varrho\nabla_{x}\Phi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(v-u)Fdv,\\
&\Delta_{x}\Phi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}Fdv-\varrho.\end{aligned}\right.(1.1)

In this system, the distribution function of the dilute charged particles is given byF​(t,x,v)F(t,x,v)with the positionx=(x1,x2,x3)∈ℝ3x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}, velocityv=(v1,v2,v3)∈ℝ3v=(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}and timet∈ℝ+t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}. The fluid is characterized by its densityϱ=ϱ​(t,x)≥0\varrho=\varrho(t,x)\geq 0, velocityu=u​(t,x)u=u(t,x), and pressurep​(ϱ)=ϱγp(\varrho)=\varrho^{\gamma}withγ≥1\gamma\geq 1.Φ​(t,x)\Phi(t,x)is the electrostatic potential. The kinetic and fluid equations are coupled through two mechanisms, namely the mutual friction forceFf:=cf​(v−u)F_{f}:=c_{f}(v-u)dependent on the relative velocity with the friction coefficientcf=1c_{f}=1, and the electrostatic force derived from the electrostatic potentialΦ​(t,x)\Phi(t,x)governed by the Poisson equation(1.1)4\eqref{introe1}_{4}.

There have been a lot of works on the kinetic-fluid system where the two phases are coupled through the friction force. For the Vlasov-Fokker-Planck-Navier-Stokes system, the global existence of weak solutions was studied in[28], and the asymptotic analysis of the solutions was studied in[29]. Moreover, the global existence and large time behavior of the strong solution when the friction coefficientcf=ϱc_{f}=\varrhowere studied in[14], and the global inviscid limit was studied in[15]. The globally nonlinear time-asymptotical stability of the planar rarefaction wave was studied in[18]. For the Vlasov-Fokker-Planck-Euler system, the global well-posedness of the Cauchy problem and rates of convergence of solutions toward equilibrium were studied in[7]for the compressible case, and the incompressible case in[5]. The global existence of weak solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck-Navier-Stokes system with arbitrary large initial data was established in[6].

Spectrum analysis is an important method for the study of PDEs. For the VPFP system, the spectrum structure and the optimal time decay rates of the classic solution to the Cauchy problem were investigated in[17], and based on this result, the corresponding Green’s function and pointwise behavior was established in[35]. For the Vlasov-Poisson(Maxwell)-Boltzmann system, the spectrum analysis and the optimal time decay rates of the global solution were studied in[20,21,22]respectively.

where0<C1≤C20<C_{1}\leq C_{2}are two generic constants.

## Remark 1.3.

Forγ=1\gamma=1, the electric field∇xΦ\nabla_{x}\Phidoes not achieve the optimal time decay rate. In fact, as noted in Remark3.7, spectrum analysis shows that the linear part of∇xΦ\nabla_{x}\Phidecays rapidly at least as(1+t)−74(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{4}}. However, by higher-order energy estimate (4.14), the nonlinear terms lead to a slower decay rate(1+t)−54(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}, as shown in (4.24).

## Remark 1.4.

In the study of the NSP and the Vlasov-Poisson-Boltzmann (VPB) systems[16,19,20], the self-consistent electric field is known to significantly affect the large time behavior of the solution. A common feature of these systems is that the Poisson equation is driven by a single density, namely, the fluid densityρ\rhoin NSP and the macroscopic densityτa​f\tau_{a}fof particles in VPB. In both cases, the resulting electric field generates the low-frequency nonlocal effects and breaks the usual acoustic wave propagation in the classical Navier Stokes (NS) and Boltzmann equations without electric field coupling[24,25]. More precisely, detailed spectrum analysis and Green’s function show that, for the NSP system, the electric field affects the dispersion of fluid and destroys the usual acoustic wave propagation in the classical compressible viscous fluids, leading to the invalidity of Huygens’ principle, and consequently a reduced time decay rate(1+t)−14(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{4}}for both momentum and electric field. For the VPB system, the same slow decay rate is also obtained for the electric field and macroscopic momentumτb​f\tau_{b}fof particles, and the Huygens’ type sound wave propagation cannot be observed.

By contrast, the VPFP system, which describes the time evolution of charged particles subject to Brownian motion, exhibits a spectrum structure different from that of the linear Fokker-Planck (FP) equation. For the FP equation, the low-frequency, spectrum consists of a single eigenvalue with the asymptotic expansionλ​(s)=−s2+O​(s3)\lambda(s)=-s^{2}+O(s^{3}), which reveals the diffusion behavior and yields the time decay rate(1+t)−34(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}for the solution[26]. For the VPFP system, the Poisson equation provides additional macroscopic density dissipation‖τa​f‖Lx22\|\tau_{a}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}, and the solution decays exponentially at the ratee−η​te^{-\eta t}forη>0\eta>0, which corresponds to the spectrum gap revealed by spectrum analysis in[17].

Different from the results above, the large time behavior of the global solution to the VPFP/NSP system established in Theorem1.2shows the solutionU=(f,ρ,u)U=(f,\rho,u)decays at the rate(1+t)−34(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}, which is consistent with the decay rates for the NS and FP equations without electric field coupling[26,27,32], while the electric field∇xΦ\nabla_{x}\Phidecays at a faster rate(1+t)−54(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}.

## Remark 1.5.

The relative velocityτb​f−u\tau_{b}f-uinduced by the friction force coupling decays at a faster rate(1+t)−54(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}, and the corresponding damping‖τb​f−u‖Hx32\|\tau_{b}f-u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}essentially dissipates the relative motion between particles and fluid and drives them toward synchronization. Furthermore, in the energy estimates, unlike[5], we do not need to directly construct the viscous dissipation‖∇xτb​f‖Hx22\|\nabla_{x}\tau_{b}f\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}of particles. Indeed, the inequality in (4.11) yields the following relation‖∇xu‖Hx32+‖τb​f−u‖Hx32≥12​‖∇xτb​f‖Hx22,\|\nabla_{x}u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}+\|\tau_{b}f-u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{x}\tau_{b}f\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}},

which reveals that the viscous dissipation‖∇xτb​f‖Hx22\|\nabla_{x}\tau_{b}f\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}can be compensated by the high-order fluid viscous dissipation‖∇xu‖Hx32\|\nabla_{x}u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}from the NS equations and the damping‖τb​f−u‖Hx32\|\tau_{b}f-u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}. Conversely, for the coupling with the Euler equations, the viscous dissipation of fluid is‖∇xu‖Hx22\|\nabla_{x}u\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}instead of‖∇xu‖Hx32\|\nabla_{x}u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}, and it can be obtained from‖∇xτb​f‖Hx22\|\nabla_{x}\tau_{b}f\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}of particles through damping‖τb​f−u‖Hx32\|\tau_{b}f-u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}as established in[5,7]. This indicates that the damping associated with the relative velocity can transfer the existing viscous dissipation between particles and fluid.

## Remark 1.6.

The coupling of the self-consistent Poisson equationΔx​Φ=τa​f−ρ\Delta_{x}\Phi=\tau_{a}f-\rhois governed by the difference between the macroscopic densityτa​f\tau_{a}fof particles and fluid densityρ\rho, leading to distinct dynamics not observed in either individual system. More precisely, the detailed asymptotic expansions of the eigenvectorsΨj​(s,ω)=(ψj,ζj,ϑj)​(s,ω)​(j=−1,0,1,2)\Psi_{j}(s,\omega)=(\psi_{j},\zeta_{j},\vartheta_{j})(s,\omega)~(j=-1,0,1,2)given in Theorem2.12reveal thatτa​ψ±1−ζ±1=O​(s2);τa​ψj−ζj≡0(j=0,2),\tau_{a}\psi_{\pm 1}-\zeta_{\pm 1}=O(s^{2});\quad\tau_{a}\psi_{j}-\zeta_{j}\equiv 0\quad(j=0,2),

which indicates that the source term of the Poisson equation is supported only on the acoustic branchesλ±1​(s)\lambda_{\pm 1}(s)fors≤r0s\leq r_{0}. Combined the expansions above with the decomposition of the semigroup in Theorem3.4, the low-frequency nonlocal effect coming from the operator∇xΔx−1\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}^{-1}vanishes as shown in (3.39), and consequently the electric field does not destroy the acoustic wave propagation. The macroscopic momentumτb​f\tau_{b}fand the fluid velocityuudecay at the rate(1+t)−3/4(1+t)^{-3/4}, while the electric field decays faster at the rate(1+t)−5/4(1+t)^{-5/4}. These large time behaviors are completely different from those of solutions to the NSP, VPB, and VPFP systems. Moreover, the source termτa​f−ρ\tau_{a}f-\rhodecays at the rate(1+t)−5/4(1+t)^{-5/4}.

We now briefly summarize the main novelties of this work, outline the main ideas of the proofs of the above theorems, and make several related comments. The present VPFP/NSP system is not a simple combination of the individual VPFP and NSP systems. Instead, the coupling mechanisms, namely the mutual friction force and the self-consistent Poisson equation, synchronize the motions of the two phases and eliminate the low-frequency nonlocal effects of the electric field, restoring the hyperbolic wave propagation behavior of classical compressible fluids.

To the best of our knowledge, this paper presents the first spectrum analysis for the kinetic-fluid-Poisson system. More precisely, the detailed spectrum structure and eigenvectors of the linearized VPFP/NSP operator𝕄​(ξ)\mathbb{M}(\xi)defined in (2.2) are established in Theorem2.12. For the structure of the spectrumσ​(𝕄​(ξ))\sigma(\mathbb{M}(\xi)), there exist four eigenvaluesλj​(|ξ|)​(j=−1,0,1,2)\lambda_{j}(|\xi|)~(j=-1,0,1,2)at low frequency and they admit the following asymptotic expansions for|ξ|≤r0|\xi|\leq r_{0}{λ±1​(|ξ|)=±i​γ+12​|ξ|−12​|ξ|2+O​(|ξ|3),λ0​(|ξ|)=λ2​(|ξ|)=−34​|ξ|2+O​(|ξ|3),\left\{\begin{aligned} \lambda_{\pm 1}(|\xi|)&=\pm i\sqrt{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}|\xi|-\frac{1}{2}|\xi|^{2}+O(|\xi|^{3}),\\
\lambda_{0}(|\xi|)&=\lambda_{2}(|\xi|)=-\frac{3}{4}|\xi|^{2}+O(|\xi|^{3}),\end{aligned}\right.

which capture both the hyperbolic wave propagation and diffusion behaviors, a phenomenon that has not been observed in the spectrum analysis of the individual VPFP and NSP systems before[16,17]. Moreover, there is a spectral gap at high frequency, namely, there existsβ=β​(r0)>0\beta=\beta(r_{0})>0such thatσ​(𝕄​(ξ))⊂{λ∈ℂ∣Re​λ<−β},|ξ|≥r0.\sigma(\mathbb{M}(\xi))\subset\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\mid\mathrm{Re}\lambda<-\beta\},\quad|\xi|\geq r_{0}.

Note that the existence of the eigenvaluesλ±1​(s)\lambda_{\pm 1}(s)cannot be obtained directly via the implicit function theorem, since the corresponding eigenvalue equationD1​(λ,s)=0D_{1}(\lambda,s)=0satisfies∂∂λ​D1​(0,0)=0\frac{\partial}{\partial_{\lambda}}D_{1}(0,0)=0. To overcome this degeneracy, the Weierstrass preparation theorem in several complex variables stated in Theorem5.7is applied to establish the analyticity and asymptotic expansions ofλ±1​(s)\lambda_{\pm 1}(s).
Based on the spectrum structure above, the decomposition of the semigroupet​𝕄​(ξ)e^{t\mathbb{M}(\xi)}is obtained in Theorem3.4, from which we obtain the optimal time decay rates of solution to the linearized VPFP/NSP system (2.1) in Theorem3.6.

For the original nonlinear problem, we establish a uniform energy estimate motivated by[4,7]. Combining Duhamel’s principle with the energy estimate, we derive the large time behavior of the solution to the original nonlinear problem, which can be proved to be optimal. To overcome the difficulties resulting from the high-order derivative term∇vf\nabla_{v}fand the velocity weighted termv​fvfin the nonlinear termR1R_{1}, we introduce the Fokker-Planck equation with damping (4.15) from[35], for which the explicit expression of Green’s function has been established, and thereby derive Lemmas4.6and4.7. These results further yield a decay rate(1+1/t)​(1+t)−34(1+1/\sqrt{t})(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}for∇vf\nabla_{v}fin a suitable weighted velocity spaceLv,2∞​(Lx2)L^{\infty}_{v,2}(L^{2}_{x}), and(1+t)−34(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}forffin the spaceLv,3∞​(Lx2)L^{\infty}_{v,3}(L^{2}_{x}), thereby closing the a priori assumption on the large time behavior of the solution.

The rest of this paper will be organized as follows: in Section 2, we study the spectrum and resolvent of the linear VPFP/NSP operator𝕄​(ξ)\mathbb{M}(\xi)and establish the asymptotic expansions of the eigenvalues and eigenfunctions at low frequency. In Section 3, we decompose the semigroupet​𝕄​(ξ)e^{t\mathbb{M}(\xi)}generated by the linear operator𝕄​(ξ)\mathbb{M}(\xi)with respect to the low and high frequency, and thus establish the optimal time decay rates of the global solution to the linearized VPFP/NSP system in terms of the semigroupet​𝕄​(ξ)e^{t\mathbb{M}(\xi)}. In Section 4, we establish the global existence and the optimal time decay rates of the solution to the original nonlinear VPFP/NSP system. Some useful lemmas and the local existence to the original nonlinear VPFP/NSP system are provided in Section 5 for the convenience of the readers.

Since|ξn|∈[r0,r1]|\xi_{n}|\in[r_{0},r_{1}],|ρn|+|un|≤1|\rho_{n}|+|u_{n}|\leq 1, there exists a subsequence{ξnj,ρnj,unj}\{\xi_{n_{j}},\rho_{n_{j}},u_{n_{j}}\}such that(ξnj,ρnj,unj)→(ξ0,ρ0,u0)(\xi_{n_{j}},\rho_{n_{j}},u_{n_{j}})\to(\xi_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0})asj→∞j\to\infty. Since|Im​λn|≤y1|\mathrm{Im}\lambda_{n}|\leq y_{1}andRe​λn→0\mathrm{Re}\lambda_{n}\to 0, there exists a subsequence{λnj}⊂{λn}\{\lambda_{n_{j}}\}\subset\{\lambda_{n}\}such thatλnj→λ0\lambda_{n_{j}}\to\lambda_{0}withRe​λ0=0\mathrm{Re}\lambda_{0}=0. Combining the results above and (2.17), we obtain𝕄​(ξ0)​U0=λ0​U0\mathbbm{M}(\xi_{0})U_{0}=\lambda_{0}U_{0}withU0=(f0,ρ0,u0)∈L2​(ℝv3)×ℂ×ℂ3U_{0}=(f_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}. Thus,λ0\lambda_{0}is an eigenvalue𝕄​(ξ0)\mathbbm{M}(\xi_{0})withRe​λ0=0\mathrm{Re}\lambda_{0}=0, which contradicts the fact thatRe​λ<0\mathrm{Re}\lambda<0forξ≠0\xi\neq 0obtained by Lemma2.3. The proof of Lemma is completed.
∎

We study the spectrum and resolvent sets of𝕄​(ξ)\mathbbm{M}(\xi)in the low-frequency regime. LetPAP_{A},PBP_{B}are the orthogonal macro-micro projection operators inL2​(ℝ3)×ℂ×ℂ3L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}defined byPA=(Pd1I3),PB=(Pr00).P_{A}=\begin{pmatrix}P_{d}&&\\

0&0&-i\xi^{T}\\
i\frac{\xi}{|\xi|^{2}}\tau_{a}&-i\xi(\gamma+\frac{1}{|\xi|^{2}})&-(1+|\xi|^{2})I_{3}\end{pmatrix},(2.18)G4​(ξ)\displaystyle G_{4}(\xi)=(L−i​Pr​(v⋅ξ)​Pr00000000),\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}L-iP_{r}(v\cdot\xi)P_{r}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix},(2.19)G5​(ξ)\displaystyle G_{5}(\xi)=(−i​(v⋅ξ)​(1+1|ξ|2)​Pd−i​Pd​(v⋅ξ)​Pri​v⋅ξ|ξ|2​χ0v​χ0000τb00).\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}-i(v\cdot\xi)(1+\frac{1}{|\xi|^{2}})P_{d}-iP_{d}(v\cdot\xi)P_{r}&i\frac{v\cdot\xi}{|\xi|^{2}}\chi_{0}&v\chi_{0}\\
0&0&0\\
\tau_{b}&0&0\end{pmatrix}.(2.20)

## Lemma 2.6.

Letξ≠0\xi\neq 0. We have the following properties:

(1) Forλ≠η~j\lambda\neq\tilde{\eta}_{j}, the operatorλ−G3​(ξ)\lambda-G_{3}(\xi)is invertible onN0×ℂ×ℂ3N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}satisfying‖(λ−G3​(ξ))−1​PA‖ξ,γ\displaystyle\|(\lambda-G_{3}(\xi))^{-1}P_{A}\|_{\xi,\gamma}≤max−1≤j≤3⁡1|λ−η~j|,\displaystyle\leq\max\limits_{-1\leq j\leq 3}\frac{1}{|\lambda-\tilde{\eta}_{j}|},(2.21)‖G5​(ξ)​(λ−G3​(ξ))−1​PA‖ξ,γ\displaystyle\|G_{5}(\xi)(\lambda-G_{3}(\xi))^{-1}P_{A}\|_{\xi,\gamma}≤C​(1+|ξ|)​max−1≤j≤3⁡1|λ−η~j|.\displaystyle\leq C(1+|\xi|)\max\limits_{-1\leq j\leq 3}\frac{1}{|\lambda-\tilde{\eta}_{j}|}.(2.22)

whereη~j,j=−1,0,1,2,3\tilde{\eta}_{j},~j=-1,0,1,2,3are the eigenvalues ofG3​(ξ)G_{3}(\xi)given byη~0=η~2=−(1+|ξ|2),η~±1=−12​(1+|ξ|2)±12​|ξ|4+(2−4​γ)​|ξ|2−3,η~3=0.\tilde{\eta}_{0}=\tilde{\eta}_{2}=-(1+|\xi|^{2}),\quad\tilde{\eta}_{\pm 1}=-\frac{1}{2}(1+|\xi|^{2})\pm\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{|\xi|^{4}+(2-4\gamma)|\xi|^{2}-3},\quad\tilde{\eta}_{3}=0.(2.23)

(2) IfRe​λ>−1\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1, then the operatorλ−G4​(ξ)\lambda-G_{4}(\xi)is invertible onN0⟂×{0}×{0}N_{0}^{\perp}\times\{0\}\times\{0\}satisfying‖(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​PB‖\displaystyle\|(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}P_{B}\|≤(1+Re​λ)−1,\displaystyle\leq(1+\mathrm{Re}\lambda)^{-1},(2.24)‖G5​(ξ)​(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​PB‖ξ,γ\displaystyle\|G_{5}(\xi)(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}P_{B}\|_{\xi,\gamma}≤C​(1+|λ|)−1​[1+(1+Re​λ)−1]​(1+|ξ|)2.\displaystyle\leq C(1+|\lambda|)^{-1}[1+(1+\mathrm{Re}\lambda)^{-1}](1+|\xi|)^{2}.(2.25)

## Proof.

We can prove (2.21) by the same way as (2.6). Indeed, we can solve the eigenvaluesη~j=ηj\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\eta_{j}(−1≤j≤2)(-1\leq j\leq 2)andη~3=0\tilde{\eta}_{3}=0viadet(λ​I5−G3​(ξ))=λ​det(λ​I4−B1​(ξ))\det(\lambda I_{5}-G_{3}(\xi))=\lambda\det(\lambda I_{4}-B_{1}(\xi)), and the corresponding eigenvectorsβ~j=(0,βj),−1≤j≤2;β~3=((1+γ​|ξ|2)​χ0,1,0),\tilde{\beta}_{j}=(0,\beta_{j}),~-1\leq j\leq 2;\quad\tilde{\beta}_{3}=((1+\gamma|\xi|^{2})\chi_{0},1,0),

whereηj,βj​(−1≤j≤2)\eta_{j},\beta_{j}~(-1\leq j\leq 2)are given by (2.8). It is easy to verify that the eigenvectorsβ~j​(−1≤j≤3)\tilde{\beta}_{j}~(-1\leq j\leq 3)satisfy(β~i,β~j)ξ,γ=0,−1≤i≠j≤3.(\tilde{\beta}_{i},\tilde{\beta}_{j})_{\xi,\gamma}=0,\quad-1\leq i\neq j\leq 3.

By Lemma5.4, we obtain‖(λ−G3​(ξ))−1​U‖ξ,γ2=∑j=−131|λ−η~j|2​1‖β~j‖ξ,γ2​|(U,β~j)ξ,γ|2≤max−1≤j≤3⁡1|λ−η~j|2​‖U‖ξ,γ2.\|(\lambda-G_{3}(\xi))^{-1}U\|^{2}_{\xi,\gamma}=\sum\limits_{j=-1}^{3}\frac{1}{|\lambda-\tilde{\eta}_{j}|^{2}}\frac{1}{\|\tilde{\beta}_{j}\|^{2}_{\xi,\gamma}}|(U,\tilde{\beta}_{j})_{\xi,\gamma}|^{2}\leq\max\limits_{-1\leq j\leq 3}\frac{1}{|\lambda-\tilde{\eta}_{j}|^{2}}\|U\|^{2}_{\xi,\gamma}.

Now we want to prove (2.22). For anyU=(f,ρ,u)∈N0×ℂ×ℂ3U=(f,\rho,u)\in N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}, we haveG5​(ξ)​U=(−i​(v⋅ξ)​f−i​(v⋅ξ)​|ξ|−2​χ0​(τa​f−ρ)+v​χ0⋅u,0,0)T,G_{5}(\xi)U=(-i(v\cdot\xi)f-i(v\cdot\xi)|\xi|^{-2}\chi_{0}(\tau_{a}f-\rho)+v\chi_{0}\cdot u,0,0)^{T},

which gives rise to‖G5​(ξ)​U‖ξ,γ≤|ξ|​‖f‖+1|ξ|​|τa​f−ρ|+|u|≤(1+|ξ|)​‖U‖ξ,γ.\|G_{5}(\xi)U\|_{\xi,\gamma}\leq|\xi|\|f\|+\frac{1}{|\xi|}|\tau_{a}f-\rho|+|u|\leq(1+|\xi|)\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}.(2.26)

Combined with (2.21), we can prove (2.22).

For anyU=(f,0,0)U=(f,0,0)withf∈N0⊥f\in N_{0}^{\bot}, we haveG5​(ξ)​U=(−i​Pd​(v⋅ξ)​f,0,τb​f)TG_{5}(\xi)U=\left(-iP_{d}(v\cdot\xi)f,0,\tau_{b}f\right)^{T}, which leads to‖G5​(ξ)​U‖ξ,γ≤(1+1|ξ|)​‖Pd​(v⋅ξ)​f‖+|τb​f|≤C​(1+|ξ|)​‖U‖ξ,γ.\|G_{5}(\xi)U\|_{\xi,\gamma}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)\|P_{d}(v\cdot\xi)f\|+|\tau_{b}f|\leq C(1+|\xi|)\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}.(2.27)

LetQ​(ξ)=L−i​Pr​(v⋅ξ)​PrQ(\xi)=L-iP_{r}(v\cdot\xi)P_{r}. By the same argument as Lemma 3.5 in[21], we have thatλ−Q​(ξ)\lambda-Q(\xi)is invertible onN0⟂N_{0}^{\perp}and satisfies that‖(λ−Q​(ξ))−1‖≤(1+Re​λ)−1\|(\lambda-Q(\xi))^{-1}\|\leq(1+\mathrm{Re}\lambda)^{-1}forRe​λ>−1\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1.
By (2.27) and using the fact that(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​U=((λ−Q​(ξ))−1​f,0,0)T,U=(f,0,0),(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}U=((\lambda-Q(\xi))^{-1}f,0,0)^{T},\quad U=(f,0,0),

we obtain (2.24) and‖G5​(ξ)​(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​U‖ξ,γ≤C​(1+|ξ|)​(1+Re​λ)−1​‖U‖ξ,γ.\|G_{5}(\xi)(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}U\|_{\xi,\gamma}\leq C(1+|\xi|)(1+\mathrm{Re}\lambda)^{-1}\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}.(2.28)

On the other hand, we decompose(λ−G4​(ξ))−1=λ−1+λ−1​G4​(ξ)​(λ−G4​(ξ))−1.(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}=\lambda^{-1}+\lambda^{-1}G_{4}(\xi)(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}.

Noticing thatG5​(ξ)​G4​(ξ)​U=(−i​Pd​(v⋅ξ)​Q​(ξ)​f,0,τb​Q​(ξ)​f)T,G_{5}(\xi)G_{4}(\xi)U=(-iP_{d}(v\cdot\xi)Q(\xi)f,0,\tau_{b}Q(\xi)f)^{T},we have‖G5​(ξ)​G4​(ξ)​U‖ξ,γ≤C​(1+|ξ|)2​‖U‖ξ,γ.\|G_{5}(\xi)G_{4}(\xi)U\|_{\xi,\gamma}\leq C(1+|\xi|)^{2}\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}.

Combined with the formula above, we obtain‖G5​(ξ)​(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​PB‖ξ,γ≤C​|λ|−1​[1+(1+Re​λ)−1]​(1+|ξ|)2.\|G_{5}(\xi)(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}P_{B}\|_{\xi,\gamma}\leq C|\lambda|^{-1}[1+(1+\mathrm{Re}\lambda)^{-1}](1+|\xi|)^{2}.(2.29)

Combining (2.28) and (2.29), we obtain (2.25). The proof of the lemma is completed.

## Lemma 2.7.

For allξ≠0\xi\neq 0, there exists a constanty2>0y_{2}>0such that{λ∈ℂ∣Re​λ>−1/2,|Im​λ|≥y2}∪{λ∈ℂ∣Re​λ>0}⊂ρ​(𝕄​(ξ)).\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\mid{\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1/2},~|\mathrm{Im}\lambda|\geq y_{2}\}\cup\left\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\mid\mathrm{Re}\lambda>0\right\}\subset\rho(\mathbbm{M}(\xi)).(2.30)

## Proof.

By Lemma2.6, we have forRe​λ>−1\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1andλ≠η~j​(ξ)​(−1≤j≤3)\lambda\neq{\tilde{\eta}_{j}(\xi)}~(-1\leq j\leq 3), the operatorλ​PA−G3​(ξ)+λ​PB−G4​(ξ)\lambda P_{A}-G_{3}(\xi)+\lambda P_{B}-G_{4}(\xi)is invertible onZξ,γZ_{\xi,\gamma}satisfying(λ​PA−G3​(ξ)+λ​PB−G4​(ξ))−1=(λ−G3​(ξ))−1​PA+(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​PB,(\lambda P_{A}-G_{3}(\xi)+\lambda P_{B}-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}=(\lambda-G_{3}(\xi))^{-1}P_{A}+(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}P_{B},

because the operatorλ​PA−G3​(ξ)\lambda P_{A}-G_{3}(\xi)is orthogonal toλ​PB−G4​(ξ)\lambda P_{B}-G_{4}(\xi). Therefore,λ−𝕄​(ξ)=\displaystyle\lambda-\mathbbm{M}(\xi)=[I−Y1​(λ,ξ)]​[λ​PA−G3​(ξ)+λ​PB−G4​(ξ)],\displaystyle\,[I-Y_{1}(\lambda,\xi)][\lambda P_{A}-G_{3}(\xi)+\lambda P_{B}-G_{4}(\xi)],Y1​(λ,ξ):=\displaystyle Y_{1}(\lambda,\xi):=G5​(ξ)​[(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​PB+(λ−G3​(ξ))−1​PA].\displaystyle\,G_{5}(\xi)[(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}P_{B}+(\lambda-G_{3}(\xi))^{-1}P_{A}].(2.31)

As shown in the proof of the lemma2.5, there exists a large constantr1>ζ​(γ)>1r_{1}>\zeta(\gamma)>1such that{λ∈ℂ∣Re​λ>−1/2}⊂ρ​(𝕄​(ξ)),|ξ|>r1.\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\mid{\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1/2}\}\subset\rho(\mathbbm{M}(\xi)),\quad|\xi|>r_{1}.

For|ξ|≤r1|\xi|\leq r_{1}, by Lemma2.6, we can choosey2>0y_{2}>0to obtain that forRe​λ>−1\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1and|Im​λ|≥y2|\mathrm{Im}\lambda|\geq y_{2},‖G5​(ξ)​(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​PB‖ξ,γ≤14,‖G5​(ξ)​(λ−G3​(ξ))−1​PA‖ξ,γ≤14,\|G_{5}(\xi)(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}P_{B}\|_{\xi,\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{4},\quad\|G_{5}(\xi)(\lambda-G_{3}(\xi))^{-1}P_{A}\|_{\xi,\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{4},(2.32)

which impliesI−Y1​(λ,ξ)I-Y_{1}(\lambda,\xi)is invertible onZξ,γZ_{\xi,\gamma}and thusλ−𝕄​(ξ)\lambda-\mathbbm{M}(\xi)is also invertible onZξ,γZ_{\xi,\gamma}satisfying(λ−𝕄​(ξ))−1=[(λ−G3​(ξ))−1​PA+(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​PB]​[I−Y1​(λ,ξ)]−1.(\lambda-\mathbbm{M}(\xi))^{-1}=[(\lambda-G_{3}(\xi))^{-1}P_{A}+(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}P_{B}][I-Y_{1}(\lambda,\xi)]^{-1}.(2.33)

Therefore, we obtain{λ∈ℂ∣Re​λ>−1/2,|Im​y|≥y2}⊂ρ​(𝕄​(ξ)),|ξ|≤r1.\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\mid\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1/2,~|\mathrm{Im}y|\geq y_{2}\}\subset\rho(\mathbbm{M}(\xi)),\quad|\xi|\leq r_{1}.

This and Lemma2.1lead to (2.30). The proof of lemma is completed.

## 2.2Low Frequency Asymptotics of Eigenvalues

We study the asymptotics of the eigenvalue and eigenfunction of the operator𝕄​(ξ)\mathbbm{M}(\xi)for low frequency. For anyU=(f,ρ,u)∈Zξ,γU=(f,\rho,u)\in Z_{\xi,\gamma}, the eigenvalue problemλ​U=𝕄​(ξ)​U\lambda U=\mathbbm{M(\xi)}Ucan be written as{λ​f=L​f−i​(v⋅ξ)​f−i​(v⋅ξ)​|ξ|−2​(Pd​f−ρ​χ0)+v​χ0⋅u,λ​ρ=−i​ξ⋅u,λ​u=i​ξ​|ξ|−2​τa​f+τb​f−i​ξ​(γ+|ξ|−2)​ρ−(1+|ξ|2)​u.\left\{\begin{aligned} \lambda f&=Lf-i(v\cdot\xi)f-i(v\cdot\xi)|\xi|^{-2}(P_{d}f-\rho\chi_{0})+v\chi_{0}\cdot u,\\
\lambda\rho&=-i\xi\cdot u,\\
\lambda u&=i\xi|\xi|^{-2}\tau_{a}f+\tau_{b}f-i\xi(\gamma+|\xi|^{-2})\rho-(1+|\xi|^{2})u.\end{aligned}\right.(2.34)

By macro-micro decompositionf=f0+f1:=Pd​f+Pr​ff=f_{0}+f_{1}:=P_{d}f+P_{r}fwithPd​f=C0​χ0P_{d}f=C_{0}\chi_{0}andPr​f=(I−Pd)​fP_{r}f=(I-P_{d})f,(2.34)1\eqref{lfae1}_{1}can be divided into{λ​f0=−i​Pd​(v⋅ξ)​(f0+f1)=−i​Pd​(v⋅ξ)​f1,λ​f1=L​f1−i​Pr​(v⋅ξ)​(f0+f1)−i​(v⋅ξ)​|ξ|−2​(f0−ρ​χ0)+v​χ0⋅u.\left\{{\allowdisplaybreaks\begin{aligned} \lambda f_{0}&=-iP_{d}(v\cdot\xi)(f_{0}+f_{1})=-iP_{d}(v\cdot\xi)f_{1},\\
\lambda f_{1}&=Lf_{1}-iP_{r}(v\cdot\xi)(f_{0}+f_{1})-i(v\cdot\xi)|\xi|^{-2}(f_{0}-\rho\chi_{0})+v\chi_{0}\cdot u.\end{aligned}}\right.(2.35)

Thus, the microscopic partf1f_{1}can be represented byf1\displaystyle f_{1}=R​(λ,ξ)​[i​(v⋅ξ)​f0+i​(v⋅ξ)​|ξ|−2​(f0−ρ​χ0)−v​χ0⋅u]\displaystyle=R(\lambda,\xi)[i(v\cdot\xi)f_{0}+i(v\cdot\xi)|\xi|^{-2}(f_{0}-\rho\chi_{0})-v\chi_{0}\cdot u]=[i​ξ​(1+|ξ|−2)​C0−i​ξ​|ξ|−2​ρ−u]⋅R​(λ,ξ)​v​χ0,\displaystyle=[i\xi(1+|\xi|^{-2})C_{0}-i\xi|\xi|^{-2}\rho-u]\cdot R(\lambda,\xi)v\chi_{0},

whereR​(λ,ξ)=[L−λ−i​Pr​(v⋅ξ)]−1,Re​λ>−1.R(\lambda,\xi)=\left[L-\lambda-iP_{r}(v\cdot\xi)\right]^{-1},\quad\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1.

Substitutingf1f_{1}into(2.34)1\eqref{lfae1}_{1}and(2.34)3\eqref{lfae1}_{3}, we obtain{λ​C0=[ξ​(1+|ξ|−2)​C0−ξ​|ξ|−2​ρ+i​u]⋅(R​(λ,ξ)​v​χ0,(v⋅ξ)​χ0),λ​ρ=−i​ξ⋅u,λ​u=i​|ξ|−2​[ξ+(1+|ξ|2)​(R​(λ,ξ)​(v⋅ξ)​χ0,v​χ0)]​C0−i​|ξ|−2​[ξ​(1+γ​|ξ|2)+(R​(λ,ξ)​(v⋅ξ)​χ0,v​χ0)]​ρ−[1+|ξ|2+(R​(λ,ξ)​v​χ0,v​χ0)]​u.\left\{\begin{aligned} \lambda C_{0}&=[\xi(1+|\xi|^{-2})C_{0}-\xi|\xi|^{-2}\rho+iu]\cdot(R(\lambda,\xi)v\chi_{0},(v\cdot\xi)\chi_{0}),\\
\lambda\rho&=-i\xi\cdot u,\\
\lambda u&=i|\xi|^{-2}\left[\xi+(1+|\xi|^{2})(R(\lambda,\xi)(v\cdot\xi)\chi_{0},v\chi_{0})\right]C_{0}\\
&\quad-i|\xi|^{-2}\left[\xi(1+\gamma|\xi|^{2})+(R(\lambda,\xi)(v\cdot\xi)\chi_{0},v\chi_{0})\right]\rho\\
&\quad-\left[1+|\xi|^{2}+(R(\lambda,\xi)v\chi_{0},v\chi_{0})\right]u.\end{aligned}\right.(2.36)

In order to simplify the eigenvalue problem, we introduce the following lemma.

## Lemma 2.8.

Lete1=(1,0,0)e_{1}=(1,0,0),ξ=s​ω\xi=s\omegawiths∈ℝs\in\mathbb{R},ω∈𝕊2\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}. Then, we have the following results forRe​λ>−1\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1and1≤i,j≤31\leq i,j\leq 3that(R​(λ,ξ)​χi,χj)=ωi​ωj​(R​(λ,s​e1)​χ1,χ1)+(δi​j−ωi​ωj)​(R​(λ,s​e1)​χ2,χ2).(R(\lambda,\xi)\chi_{i},\chi_{j})=\omega_{i}\omega_{j}\left(R(\lambda,se_{1})\chi_{1},\chi_{1}\right)+(\delta_{ij}-\omega_{i}\omega_{j})\left(R(\lambda,se_{1})\chi_{2},\chi_{2}\right).

## Proof.

Let𝕆\mathbb{O}be a rotation inℝ3\mathbb{R}^{3}satisfying𝕆T​ξ=(s,0,0)\mathbb{O}^{T}\xi=(s,0,0). By changing the variablev→𝕆​vv\to\mathbb{O}vand using the rotational invariance of the
operatorLL, we can prove the lemma.

For convenience of notation, denoteRi​j=Ri​j​(λ,s)=(R​(λ,s​e1)​χi,χj),i,j=1,2,3.R_{ij}=R_{ij}(\lambda,s)=(R(\lambda,se_{1})\chi_{i},\chi_{j}),\quad i,j=1,2,3.(2.37)

With the help of Lemma2.8, the eigenvalue problem (2.34) can be simplified as{λ​C0=(1+s2)​R11​C0−R11​ρ+i​s​R11​(ω⋅u),λ​ρ=−i​s​(ω⋅u),λ​u=i​ω​1s​[1+(1+s2)​R11]​C0−i​ω​1s​(1+γ​s2+R11)​ρ−R11​ω​(ω⋅u)−R22​[u−ω​(ω⋅u)]−(1+s2)​u.\left\{{\allowdisplaybreaks\begin{aligned} \lambda C_{0}&=(1+s^{2})R_{11}C_{0}-R_{11}\rho+isR_{11}(\omega\cdot u),\\
\lambda\rho&=-is(\omega\cdot u),\\
\lambda u&=i\omega\frac{1}{s}[1+(1+s^{2})R_{11}]C_{0}-i\omega\frac{1}{s}\left(1+\gamma s^{2}+R_{11}\right)\rho&\\
&\quad-R_{11}\omega(\omega\cdot u)-R_{22}[u-\omega(\omega\cdot u)]-(1+s^{2})u.\end{aligned}}\right.(2.38)

## Remark 2.9.

LetU=(f,ρ,u)U=(f,\rho,u)be the eigenvector of𝕄​(ξ)\mathbbm{M}(\xi)corresponding the eigenvalueλ\lambda. Then, the eigenvalue problemλ​U=𝕄​(ξ)​U\lambda U=\mathbbm{M}(\xi)Uis equivalent toλ​PA​U=G3​(ξ)​PA​U+G5​(ξ)​(λ−G4​(ξ))−1​G5​(ξ)​PA​U,Re​λ>−1,\lambda P_{A}U=G_{3}(\xi)P_{A}U+G_{5}(\xi)(\lambda-G_{4}(\xi))^{-1}G_{5}(\xi)P_{A}U,\quad\mathrm{Re}\lambda>-1,(2.39)

and then it is equivalent to (2.38) withPA​U=(C0​χ0,ρ,u)P_{A}U=(C_{0}\chi_{0},\rho,u).

## Remark 2.13.

In fact, for the general viscous termν​Δx​u\nu\Delta_{x}uwithν>0\nu>0in the equation(1.2)3\eqref{introe2}_{3}, the asymptotic expansions of the eigenvalues{λj​(s),j=−1,0,1,2}\{\lambda_{j}(s),~j=-1,0,1,2\}become{λ±1​(s)=±i​γ+12​s−1+ν4​s2+O​(s3),λ0​(s)=λ2​(s)=−1+2​ν4​s2+O​(s3),\left\{\begin{aligned} \lambda_{\pm 1}(s)&=\pm i\sqrt{\frac{\gamma+1}{2}}s-\frac{1+\nu}{4}s^{2}+O(s^{3}),\\
\lambda_{0}(s)&=\lambda_{2}(s)=-\frac{1+2\nu}{4}s^{2}+O(s^{3}),\end{aligned}\right.(2.59)

which can be obtained by an argument similar to the proof of Lemma2.11.

## 3Optimal Time-Decay Rates of Linearized VPFP/NSP

In the section, we consider the Cauchy problem (2.1) for the linearized VPFP/NSP system and establish the optimal time decay rates of the global solution based on the results obtained in Section 2.

## 3.1Decomposition and Asymptotics of Linear Semigroup

We start by proving the following lemmas.

## Lemma 3.1.

The operatorG3​(ξ)G_{3}(\xi)defined by (2.18) generates a strongly continuous contraction semigroup onN0×ℂ×ℂ3N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}for any fixedξ≠0\xi\neq 0, which satisfies for anyt>0t>0andU∈N0×ℂ×ℂ3U\in N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}that‖et​G3​(ξ)​U‖ξ,γ≤‖U‖ξ,γ.\|e^{tG_{3}(\xi)}U\|_{\xi,\gamma}\leq\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}.(3.1)

In addition, for anyλ=x+i​y≠0\lambda=x+iy\neq 0withx∈(−12,0)x\in(-\frac{1}{2},0)andU∈N0×ℂ×ℂ3U\in N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}, it holds that∫−∞+∞‖[(x+i​y)−G3​(ξ)]−1​U‖ξ,γ2​𝑑y≤C​‖U‖ξ,γ2.\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\|[(x+iy)-G_{3}(\xi)]^{-1}U\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}dy\leq C\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}.(3.2)

## Proof.

Note thatG3​(ξ)G_{3}(\xi)is a densely defined closed operator onN0×ℂ×ℂ3N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}, and bothG3​(ξ)G_{3}(\xi)and its adjoint operatorG3​(ξ)∗=G3​(−ξ)G_{3}(\xi)^{*}=G_{3}(-\xi)are dissipative onN0×ℂ×ℂ3N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}satisfyingRe​(G3​(ξ)​U,U)ξ,γ=Re​(G3​(ξ)∗​U,U)ξ,γ≤0.\mathrm{Re}(G_{3}(\xi)U,U)_{\xi,\gamma}=\mathrm{Re}(G_{3}(\xi)^{*}U,U)_{\xi,\gamma}\leq 0.

Thus,G3​(ξ)G_{3}(\xi)generates a strongly continuous contraction semigroup onN0×ℂ×ℂ3N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}satisfying (3.1). By Lemma2.6, we have forU∈N0×ℂ×ℂ3U\in N_{0}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3},∫−∞∞‖(λ−G3​(ξ))−1​U‖ξ,γ2​𝑑y=∫−∞∞∑j=−13‖1λ−η~j​(U,β~j)ξ,γ2​β~j‖ξ,γ2​d​y\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\|(\lambda-G_{3}(\xi))^{-1}U\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}dy=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j=-1}^{3}\left\|\frac{1}{\lambda-\tilde{\eta}_{j}}(U,\tilde{\beta}_{j})_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}\tilde{\beta}_{j}\right\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}dy≤‖U‖ξ,γ2​max−1≤j≤3​∫−∞∞1|λ−η~j|2​𝑑y=‖U‖ξ,γ2​max−1≤j≤3​∫−∞∞1(x−Re​η~j)2+(y−Im​η~j)2​𝑑y\displaystyle\leq\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}\max_{-1\leq j\leq 3}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{|\lambda-\tilde{\eta}_{j}|^{2}}dy=\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}\max_{-1\leq j\leq 3}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\left(x-\mathrm{Re}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\right)^{2}+\left(y-\mathrm{Im}\tilde{\eta}_{j}\right)^{2}}dy=‖U‖ξ,γ2​max−1≤j≤3⁡π|x−Re​η~j|≤C​‖U‖ξ,γ2.\displaystyle=\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}\max_{-1\leq j\leq 3}\frac{\pi}{|x-\mathrm{Re}\tilde{\eta}_{j}|}\leq C\|U\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}.

The proof of the lemma is completed.
∎

## Lemma 3.2.

The operatorG4​(ξ)G_{4}(\xi)defined by (2.18) generates a strongly continuous contraction semigroup onN0⟂×{0}×{0}N^{\perp}_{0}\times\{0\}\times\{0\}for any fixedξ∈ℝ3\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}, which satisfies for anyt>0t>0andU=(f,0,0)U=(f,0,0)withf∈N0⟂f\in N^{\perp}_{0}that‖et​G4​(ξ)​U‖≤e−t​‖U‖.\|e^{tG_{4}(\xi)}U\|\leq e^{-t}\|U\|.(3.3)

In addition, for anyλ=x+i​y\lambda=x+iywithx>−1x>-1andU=(f,0,0)U=(f,0,0)withf∈N0⟂f\in N^{\perp}_{0}, it holds that∫−∞+∞‖[(x+i​y)−G4​(ξ)]−1​U‖2​𝑑y≤π​(x+1)−1​‖U‖2.\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\|[(x+iy)-G_{4}(\xi)]^{-1}U\|^{2}dy\leq\pi(x+1)^{-1}\|U\|^{2}.(3.4)

## Proof.

Note that forU=(f,0,0)∈N0⟂×{0}×{0}U=(f,0,0)\in N^{\perp}_{0}\times\{0\}\times\{0\},et​G4​(ξ)​U=(et​Q​(ξ)​f,0,0)T.e^{tG_{4}(\xi)}U=(e^{tQ(\xi)}f,0,0)^{T}.

Note thatQ​(ξ)=L−i​Pr​(v⋅ξ)​PrQ(\xi)=L-iP_{r}(v\cdot\xi)P_{r}is a densely defined closed operator onN0⟂N^{\perp}_{0}, and bothQ​(ξ)Q(\xi)and its adjoint operatorQ​(ξ)∗=Q1​(−ξ)Q(\xi)^{*}=Q_{1}(-\xi)are dissipative onN0⟂N^{\perp}_{0}satisfyingRe​(Q​(ξ)​f,f)=Re​(Q​(ξ)∗​U,U)=Re​(L​f,f)≤−‖f‖2,f∈N0⟂.\mathrm{Re}(Q(\xi)f,f)=\mathrm{Re}(Q(\xi)^{*}U,U)=\mathrm{Re}(Lf,f)\leq-\|f\|^{2},\quad f\in N_{0}^{\perp}.

Thus,G4​(ξ)G_{4}(\xi)generates a strongly continuous contraction semigroup onN0⟂N^{\perp}_{0}satisfying (3.3). Moreover, we have forU=(f,0,0)∈N0⟂×{0}×{0}U=(f,0,0)\in N^{\perp}_{0}\times\{0\}\times\{0\}that[λ−G4​(ξ)]−1​U\displaystyle[\lambda-G_{4}(\xi)]^{-1}U=∫0∞e−λ​t​(et​Q​(ξ)​f,0,0)T​𝑑t\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}(e^{tQ(\xi)}f,0,0)^{T}dt=12​π​∫−∞+∞e−i​y​t​(2​π​1t≥0​e−x​t​et​G4​(ξ)​U)​𝑑t,x>−1,\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-iyt}\left(\sqrt{2\pi}1_{t\geq 0}e^{-xt}e^{tG_{4}(\xi)}U\right)dt,\quad x>-1,

where the right hand side is the Fourier transform of the function2​π​1t≥0​e−x​t​et​G4​(ξ)​U\sqrt{2\pi}1_{t\geq 0}e^{-xt}e^{tG_{4}(\xi)}Uwith respect tott. By using the Plancherel’s equality, we have forU=(f,0,0)U=(f,0,0)withf∈N0⟂∩L2​(ℝv3)f\in N^{\perp}_{0}\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})that∫−∞+∞‖[(x+i​y)−G4​(ξ)]−1​U‖2​𝑑y=∫−∞+∞‖2​π​1t≥0​e−x​t​et​G4​(ξ)​U‖2​𝑑y\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\|[(x+iy)-G_{4}(\xi)]^{-1}U\|^{2}dy=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\|\sqrt{2\pi}1_{t\geq 0}e^{-xt}e^{tG_{4}(\xi)}U\|^{2}dy=2​π​∫−∞+∞e−2​x​t​‖et​G4​(ξ)​U‖2​𝑑t≤2​π​∫0∞e−2​(x+1)​t​𝑑t​‖U‖2≤π​(x+1)−1​‖U‖2,\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-2xt}\|e^{tG_{4}(\xi)}U\|^{2}dt\leq 2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-2(x+1)t}dt\|U\|^{2}\leq\pi(x+1)^{-1}\|U\|^{2},

which proves (3.4). The proof of the lemma is completed.

## Lemma 3.3.

The operatorG1​(ξ)G_{1}(\xi)defined by (2.9) generates a strongly continuous contraction semigroup onL2​(ℝv3)×ℂ×ℂ3L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}for any fixed|ξ|≥r0>0|\xi|\geq r_{0}>0, which satisfies for anyt>0t>0andU∈L2​(ℝv3)×ℂ×ℂ3U\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}that‖et​G1​(ξ)​U‖≤C​e−12​t​‖U‖,\|e^{tG_{1}(\xi)}U\|\leq Ce^{-\frac{1}{2}t}\|U\|,(3.5)

whereC>1C>1is a constant depending onr0r_{0}. For anyx>−12x>-\frac{1}{2}andU∈L2​(ℝv3)×ℂ×ℂ3U\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}, we obtain∫−∞+∞‖[(x+i​y)−G1​(ξ)]−1​U‖2​𝑑y≤C​‖U‖2.\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\|[(x+iy)-G_{1}(\xi)]^{-1}U\|^{2}dy\leq C\|U\|^{2}.(3.6)

## Proof.

Note that forU=(f,X)∈L2​(ℝv3)×ℂ4U=(f,X)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\times\mathbb{C}^{4},et​G1​(ξ)​U=(et​A​(ξ)​f,et​B1​(ξ)​X)T.e^{tG_{1}(\xi)}U=(e^{tA(\xi)}f,e^{tB_{1}(\xi)}X)^{T}.

The operatorA​(ξ)=A−i​(v⋅ξ)A(\xi)=A-i(v\cdot\xi)generates a strongly continuous contraction semigroup onL2​(ℝv3)L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}), which satisfies for anyt>0t>0andf∈L2​(ℝv3)f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})that‖et​A​(ξ)​f‖≤e−32​t​‖f‖.\|e^{tA(\xi)}f\|\leq e^{-\frac{3}{2}t}\|f\|.(3.7)

In addition, for anyx>−32x>-\frac{3}{2}andf∈L2​(ℝv3)f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}), we can prove by the same way as (3.4) that∫−∞+∞‖[(x+i​y)−A​(ξ)]−1​f‖2​𝑑y≤π​(x+32)−1​‖f‖2.\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\|[(x+iy)-A(\xi)]^{-1}f\|^{2}dy\leq\pi\left(x+\frac{3}{2}\right)^{-1}\|f\|^{2}.(3.8)

By (5.2), we obtain for anyX∈ℂ4X\in\mathbb{C}^{4},‖et​B1​(ξ)​X‖2\displaystyle\|e^{tB_{1}(\xi)}X\|^{2}≤‖et​B1​(ξ)​X‖𝒞ξ,γ2=∑j=−12‖eηj​t​(X,βj)𝒞ξ,γ​βj‖𝒞ξ,γ2\displaystyle\leq\|e^{tB_{1}(\xi)}X\|^{2}_{\mathcal{C}_{\xi,\gamma}}=\sum\limits_{j=-1}^{2}\left\|e^{\eta_{j}t}(X,\beta_{j})_{\mathcal{C}_{\xi,\gamma}}\beta_{j}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{C}_{\xi,\gamma}}(3.9)≤C​e−t​‖X‖𝒞ξ,γ2≤C​e−t​‖X‖2,C>1.\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-t}\|X\|^{2}_{\mathcal{C}_{\xi,\gamma}}\leq Ce^{-t}\|X\|^{2},\quad C>1.

By (5.1), we obtain forλ=x+i​y\lambda=x+iywithx>−12x>-\frac{1}{2}that∫−∞∞‖(λ−B1​(ξ))−1​X‖2​𝑑y≤∫−∞∞∑j=−12‖1λ−ηj​(X,βj)𝒞ξ,γ​βj‖𝒞ξ,γ2​d​y\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\|(\lambda-B_{1}(\xi))^{-1}X\|^{2}dy\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j=-1}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\lambda-\eta_{j}}(X,\beta_{j})_{\mathcal{C}_{\xi,\gamma}}\beta_{j}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\xi,\gamma}}^{2}dy≤‖X‖𝒞ξ,γ2​max−1≤j≤2⁡π|x−Re​ηj|≤C​‖X‖𝒞ξ,γ2≤C​‖X‖2.\displaystyle\leq\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\xi,\gamma}}^{2}\max_{-1\leq j\leq 2}\frac{\pi}{|x-\mathrm{Re}\eta_{j}|}\leq C\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\xi,\gamma}}^{2}\leq C\|X\|^{2}.(3.10)

By (3.7) and (3.9), we obtain‖et​G1​(ξ)​U‖\displaystyle\|e^{tG_{1}(\xi)}U\|≤‖et​A​(ξ)​f‖+‖et​B1​(ξ)​X‖≤C​e−12​t​‖U‖,C>1.\displaystyle\leq\|e^{tA(\xi)}f\|+\|e^{tB_{1}(\xi)}X\|\leq Ce^{-\frac{1}{2}t}\|U\|,\quad C>1.

we prove (3.6) forx>−12x>-\frac{1}{2}. The proof of the lemma is completed.

By virtue of Lemmas3.1–3.3, we have the decomposition of the semigroupS​(t,ξ)=et​𝕄​(ξ)S(t,\xi)=e^{t\mathbbm{M}(\xi)}generated by𝕄​(ξ)\mathbbm{M}(\xi)as follows.

## 3.2Optimal Time-Decay Rates of Linearized VPFP/NSP

Based on the decomposition of the semigroup given in the previous subsection, we now study the optimal convergence rates of the solution of the linearized system to the equilibrium.

SetU=(f,ρ,u)U=(f,\rho,u)withf=f​(x,v)f=f(x,v),ρ=ρ​(x)\rho=\rho(x)andu=u​(x)u=u(x), denote the Banach spaceZq={U=(f,ρ,u)∈L2,q×Lxq×Lxq∣‖U‖Zq<∞}Z^{q}=\{U=(f,\rho,u)\in L^{2,q}\times L^{q}_{x}\times L^{q}_{x}\mid\|U\|_{Z^{q}}<\infty\}with the norm‖U‖Zq2=‖f‖L2,q2+‖ρ‖Lxq2+‖u‖Lxq2.\|U\|_{Z^{q}}^{2}=\|f\|_{L^{2,q}}^{2}+\|\rho\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{2}.

Define the Sobolev spaceHPlH^{l}_{P}asHPl={U=(f,ρ,u)∈Z2∣‖U‖HPl<∞}H^{l}_{P}=\{U=(f,\rho,u)\in Z^{2}\mid\|U\|_{H^{l}_{P}}<\infty\}equipped with the norm‖U‖HPl2\displaystyle\|U\|^{2}_{H^{l}_{P}}=∫ℝ3(1+|ξ|2)l​‖U^‖ξ,γ2​𝑑ξ\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{l}\|\hat{U}\|^{2}_{\xi,\gamma}d\xi=∫ℝ3(1+|ξ|2)l​(∫ℝ3|f^|2​𝑑v+γ​|ρ^|2+|u^|2+1|ξ|2​|τa​f^−ρ^|2)​𝑑ξ,\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{l}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\hat{f}|^{2}dv+\gamma|\hat{\rho}|^{2}+|\hat{u}|^{2}+\frac{1}{|\xi|^{2}}|\tau_{a}\hat{f}-\hat{\rho}|^{2}\right)d\xi,

wheref^=f^​(ξ,v)\hat{f}=\hat{f}(\xi,v),ρ^=ρ^​(ξ)\hat{\rho}=\hat{\rho}(\xi)andu^=u^​(ξ)\hat{u}=\hat{u}(\xi).

For anyU0=(f0,ρ0,u0)∈Lv2​(Hxl)×Hxl×HxlU_{0}=(f_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0})\in L^{2}_{v}(H^{l}_{x})\times H^{l}_{x}\times H^{l}_{x}, setet​𝕄​U0=(ℱ−1​et​𝕄​(ξ)​ℱ)​U0.e^{t\mathbbm{M}}U_{0}=(\mathcal{F}^{-1}e^{t\mathbbm{M}(\xi)}\mathcal{F})U_{0}.

By Lemma2.1, we have‖et​𝕄​U0‖HPl2=∫ℝ3(1+|ξ|2)l​‖et​𝕄​(ξ)​U^0‖ξ,γ2​𝑑ξ≤∫ℝ3(1+|ξ|2)l​‖U^0‖ξ,γ2​𝑑ξ=‖U0‖HPl2.\|e^{t\mathbbm{M}}U_{0}\|_{H^{l}_{P}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{l}\|e^{t\mathbbm{M}(\xi)}\hat{U}_{0}\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}d\xi\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{l}\|\hat{U}_{0}\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}d\xi=\|U_{0}\|_{H^{l}_{P}}^{2}.

This means that the linear operator𝕄\mathbbm{M}generates a strongly continuous contradiction semigroupet​𝕄e^{t\mathbbm{M}}inHPlH^{l}_{P}. Therefore,U=et​𝕄​U0U=e^{t\mathbbm{M}}U_{0}is a global solution to (2.1) for the linearized VPFP/NSP system with initial dataU0∈HPlU_{0}\in H^{l}_{P}.

In fact, the following theorem will prove that the time deca rates above are optimal.

## Theorem 3.6.

LetU​(t)=(f​(t),ρ​(t),u​(t))=et​𝕄​U0U(t)=(f(t),\rho(t),u(t))=e^{t\mathbb{M}}U_{0}be a solution of the system (2.1). Then, it holds fort>0t>0being large enough that{C1​(1+t)−34≤‖f​(t)‖Lx,v2≤C2​(1+t)−34,C1​(1+t)−34≤‖ρ​(t)‖Lx2≤C2​(1+t)−34,C1​(1+t)−34≤‖u​(t)‖Lx2≤C2​(1+t)−34,\left\{\begin{aligned} C_{1}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}\leq&\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C_{2}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}},\\

C_{1}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}\leq&\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq C_{2}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}},\end{aligned}\right.(3.42)

and forγ>1\gamma>1thatC1​(1+t)−54≤‖∇xΦ​(t)‖Lx2≤C2​(1+t)−54,C_{1}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}\leq\|\nabla_{x}\Phi(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq C_{2}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}},(3.43)

Indeed, in terms of Theorem3.5, we have‖f​(t)‖Lx,v2\displaystyle\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}≥‖h​(t)‖Lξ,v2−‖g​(t)‖Lξ,v2≥‖h​(t)‖Lξ,v2−C​e−κ0​t,\displaystyle\geq\|h(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi,v}}-\|g(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi,v}}\geq\|h(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi,v}}-Ce^{-\kappa_{0}t},(3.44)‖ρ​(t)‖Lx2\displaystyle\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}≥‖H​(t)‖Lξ2−‖G​(t)‖Lξ2≥‖G​(t)‖Lξ2−C​e−κ0​t,\displaystyle\geq\|H(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}-\|G(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\geq\|G(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}-Ce^{-\kappa_{0}t},(3.45)‖u​(t)‖Lx2\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}≥‖E​(t)‖Lξ2−‖B​(t)‖Lξ2≥‖E​(t)‖Lξ2−C​e−κ0​t,\displaystyle\geq\|E(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}-\|B(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}\geq\|E(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}-Ce^{-\kappa_{0}t},(3.46)‖∇xΦ​(t)‖Lx2\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\Phi(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}≥‖|ξ|−1​[τa​h​(t)−H​(t)]‖Lξ2−‖|ξ|−1​[τa​g​(t)−G​(t)]‖Lξ2\displaystyle\geq\||\xi|^{-1}[\tau_{a}h(t)-H(t)]\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}-\||\xi|^{-1}[\tau_{a}g(t)-G(t)]\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}≥‖|ξ|−1​[τa​h​(t)−H​(t)]‖Lξ2−C​e−κ0​t,\displaystyle\geq\||\xi|^{-1}[\tau_{a}h(t)-H(t)]\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}-Ce^{-\kappa_{0}t},(3.47)

where we have made use of (3.34)∫ℝ3‖(g​(t),G​(t),B​(t))‖ξ,γ2​𝑑ξ≤C​e−2​κ0​t​(‖U0‖Z22+‖U0‖Z12).\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|(g(t),G(t),B(t))\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}d\xi\leq Ce^{-2\kappa_{0}t}(\|U_{0}\|_{Z^{2}}^{2}+\|U_{0}\|_{Z^{1}}^{2}).

By (2.45) and (3.36), we have‖h​(t)‖Lv22≥116​e2​t​Re​λ1​(|ξ|)​|τa​f^0+ρ^0|2−C​|ξ|2​e−|ξ|2​t​‖U^0‖ξ,γ2,\|h(t)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{16}e^{2t\mathrm{Re}\lambda_{1}(|\xi|)}|\tau_{a}\hat{f}_{0}+\hat{\rho}_{0}|^{2}-C|\xi|^{2}e^{-|\xi|^{2}t}\|\hat{U}_{0}\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2},(3.48)

whereC>0C>0is a constant. Noticing that fort≥t0=1r02t\geq t_{0}=\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}, we have∫|ξ|≤r0e−|ξ|2​t​𝑑ξ=4​π​t−32​∫0r0​tr2​e−r2​𝑑r≥C​(1+t)−32,\int_{|\xi|\leq r_{0}}e^{-|\xi|^{2}t}d\xi=4\pi t^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{0}^{r_{0}\sqrt{t}}r^{2}e^{-r^{2}}dr\geq C(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}},

this together with (3.48) yeild‖h​(t)‖Lξ,v22\displaystyle\|h(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi,v}}^{2}≥116​d02​∫|ξ|≤r0e−|ξ|2​t​𝑑ξ−C​∫|ξ|≤r0|ξ|2​e−|ξ|2​t​‖U^0‖ξ,γ2​𝑑ξ\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{16}d_{0}^{2}\int_{|\xi|\leq r_{0}}e^{-|\xi|^{2}t}d\xi-C\int_{|\xi|\leq r_{0}}|\xi|^{2}e^{-|\xi|^{2}t}\|\hat{U}_{0}\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2}d\xi≥C​(1+t)−32−C​(1+t)−52.\displaystyle\geq C(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}-C(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}}.(3.49)

Similar to the discussion above, we can also obtian|E​(t)|2,|H​(t)|2\displaystyle|E(t)|^{2},|H(t)|^{2}≥116​e2​t​Re​λ1​(|ξ|)​|τa​f^0+ρ^0|2−C​|ξ|2​e−|ξ|2​t​‖U^0‖ξ,γ2,\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{16}e^{2t\mathrm{Re}\lambda_{1}(|\xi|)}|\tau_{a}\hat{f}_{0}+\hat{\rho}_{0}|^{2}-C|\xi|^{2}e^{-|\xi|^{2}t}\|\hat{U}_{0}\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2},|τa​h​(t)−H​(t)|2\displaystyle|\tau_{a}h(t)-H(t)|^{2}≥C​|ξ|4​e2​t​Re​λ1​(|ξ|)​|τa​f^0+ρ^0|2−C​|ξ|6​e−|ξ|2​t​‖U^0‖ξ,γ2,γ>1,\displaystyle\geq C|\xi|^{4}e^{2t\mathrm{Re}\lambda_{1}(|\xi|)}|\tau_{a}\hat{f}_{0}+\hat{\rho}_{0}|^{2}-C|\xi|^{6}e^{-|\xi|^{2}t}\|\hat{U}_{0}\|_{\xi,\gamma}^{2},\quad\gamma>1,

and then‖E​(t)‖Lξ22,‖H​(t)‖Lξ22\displaystyle\|E(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}^{2},\|H(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}^{2}≥C​(1+t)−32−C​(1+t)−52,\displaystyle\geq C(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}-C(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}},(3.50)‖|ξ|−1​[τa​h​(t)−H​(t)]‖Lξ22\displaystyle\||\xi|^{-1}[\tau_{a}h(t)-H(t)]\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}^{2}≥C​(1+t)−52−C​(1+t)−72,γ>1.\displaystyle\geq C(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}}-C(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{2}},\quad\gamma>1.(3.51)

Combining (3.44)-(3.47) and (3.49)-(3.51), we obtain (3.42)-(3.43) fort>0t>0large enough. The proof of theorem is completed.
∎

## Remark 3.7.

Noticing that the special caseγ=1\gamma=1leads to the vanishing of the|ξ|2|\xi|^{2}order term in (3.39). Indeed, for the general viscous termν​Δx​u\nu\Delta_{x}uin the equation(1.2)3\eqref{introe2}_{3}andγ=1\gamma=1, we obtain the following asymptotic expansion from (2.55) and (2.59) thatτa​h​(t)−H​(t)=ν−132​|ξ|3​∑j=±1et​λj​(|ξ|)​{(τa​f^0+ρ^0)−j​[(τb​f^0+u^0)⋅ω]}+O​(|ξ|4​e−12​|ξ|2​t).\tau_{a}h(t)-H(t)=\frac{\nu-1}{32}|\xi|^{3}\sum\limits_{j=\pm 1}e^{t\lambda_{j}(|\xi|)}\Big\{(\tau_{a}\hat{f}_{0}+\hat{\rho}_{0})-j[(\tau_{b}\hat{f}_{0}+\hat{u}_{0})\cdot\omega]\Big\}+O(|\xi|^{4}e^{-\frac{1}{2}|\xi|^{2}t}).

Thus, forγ=1\gamma=1andν≠1\nu\neq 1, the electric field∇xΦ\nabla_{x}\Phiadmits the following optimal time decay rateC1​(1+t)−74≤‖∇xΦ​(t)‖Lx2≤C2​(1+t)−74,C_{1}(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{4}}\leq\|\nabla_{x}\Phi(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq C_{2}(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{4}},

while forν=1\nu=1only an upper bound holds‖∇xΦ‖Lx2≤C​(1+t)−94\|\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq C(1+t)^{-\frac{9}{4}}.

## 4The Original Nonlinear Problem

In this section, we establish the global existence of the solution to the original nonlinear VPFP/NSP system (1.2), and with the help of the estimates on the linearized system obtained in Section 3, we prove the long time decay rates of the solution to this system.

## 4.1Energy Estimate

LetU=(f,ρ,u)U=(f,\rho,u)be a solution to (1.2) on0≤t≤T∗0\leq t\leq T^{*}. We assume thatsup0≤t≤T∗E3​(U)≤δ0,\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}E_{3}(U)\leq\delta_{0},(4.1)

whereδ0>0\delta_{0}>0is a sufficiently small constant.

LetU=(f,ρ,u)U=(f,\rho,u)be a strong solution to (1.2). ## Proof.

we obtain12​dd​t​(‖∂xαf‖Lx,v22+γ​‖∂xαρ‖Lx22+‖∂xαu‖Lx22+‖∂xα∇xΦ‖Lx22)\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\left(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\gamma\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)+∫ℝ3(−L​∂xαP1​f,∂xαf)​𝑑x+‖∂xα∇xu‖Lx22+‖∂xα(τb​f−u)‖Lx22:=∑k=13Jk.\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(-L\partial^{\alpha}_{x}P_{1}f,\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f)dx+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f-u)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}:=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{3}J_{k}.

J1J_{1}gives the terms as followsJ1\displaystyle J_{1}=∫ℝ3(∂xα[(∇xΦ+u)⋅(12​v​f−∇vf)],∂xαf)​𝑑x\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\left[(\nabla_{x}\Phi+u)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}vf-\nabla_{v}f\right)\right],\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f\right)dx≤C​∑1≤|α′|≤|α|−1∫ℝ3‖∂xα−α′(∇xΦ+u)‖Lx3​(‖∂xα′|​v​|f‖Lx6+‖∂xα′∇vf‖Lx6)​‖∂xαf‖Lx2​𝑑v\displaystyle\leq C\sum\limits_{1\leq|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq|\alpha|-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\nabla_{x}\Phi+u)\|_{L^{3}_{x}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}|v|f\|_{L^{6}_{x}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{6}_{x}})\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}dv+C​∫ℝ3‖∇xΦ+u‖Lx∞​(‖∂xα|​v​|f‖Lx2+‖∂xα∇vf‖Lx2)​‖∂xαf‖Lx2​𝑑x\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\nabla_{x}\Phi+u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}|v|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}})\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}dx+C​∫ℝ3‖∂xα(∇xΦ+u)‖Lx2​(‖|v|​f‖Lx∞+‖∇vf‖Lx∞)​‖∂xαf‖Lx2​𝑑x\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\nabla_{x}\Phi+u)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}(\||v|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}dx≤C​‖(u,∇xΦ)‖Hx2​‖∇xP0​f‖Lv2​(Hx2)2+C​‖(u,∇xΦ)‖Hx2​‖∇xP1​f‖Lσ2​(Hx2)2.\displaystyle\leq C\|(u,\nabla_{x}\Phi)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla_{x}P_{0}f\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}+C\|(u,\nabla_{x}\Phi)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla_{x}P_{1}f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}.

J2J_{2}gives the terms as followsJ2=−γ​∫ℝ3∂xαρ​∂xαdivx​(ρ​u)​d​x=−γ​∫ℝ3∂xαρ​∂xα(∇xρ⋅u+ρ​divx​u)​d​x:=J21+J22.J_{2}=-\gamma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho\partial^{\alpha}_{x}{\rm div}_{x}(\rho u)dx=-\gamma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\nabla_{x}\rho\cdot u+\rho{\rm div}_{x}u)dx:=J_{21}+J_{22}.

We denote[A,B]:=A​B−B​A[A,B]:=AB-BAfor two operatorsAAandBB. ## Lemma 4.4.

LetU=(f,ρ,u)U=(f,\rho,u)be a strong solution to (1.2). Then, there exists two constantsq0,q1>0q_{0},q_{1}>0such that∑|α|≤2{dd​t​∫ℝ3(∂xα∇xτa​f⋅∂xατb​f+∂xα∇xρ⋅∂xαu)​𝑑x+γ2‖∂xα∇xρ∥Lx22+q0​‖∂xα(∇xτa​f,Δx​Φ)‖Lx22}\displaystyle\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left\{\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\tau_{b}f+\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}u\right)dx+\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+q_{0}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f,\Delta_{x}\Phi)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right\}≤q1​(‖∇xP1​f‖Lσ2​(Hx2)2+‖∇x(τb​f−u)‖Hx22+‖∇xu‖Hx32)+‖divx​τb​f‖Hx22.\displaystyle\leq q_{1}\left(\|\nabla_{x}P_{1}f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}(\tau_{b}f-u)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right)+\|{\rm div}_{x}\tau_{b}f\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}.(4.9)

## Proof.

Applying∂xα\partial^{\alpha}_{x}with|α|≤2|\alpha|\leq 2to(1.2)3\eqref{introe2}_{3}and(4.7)2\eqref{ge2}_{2}, respectively, and then multiplying them by∂xα∇xρ\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rhoand∂xα∇xτa​f\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f, respectively, taking integration and using (4.8), we obtaindd​t​(∫ℝ3∂xα∇xτa​f⋅∂xατb​f​d​x+∫ℝ3∂xα∇xρ​∂xαu​d​x)+γ​‖∂xα∇xρ‖Lx22+‖∂xα(∇xτa​f,Δx​Φ)‖Lx22\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\tau_{b}fdx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\partial^{\alpha}_{x}udx\right)+\gamma\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f,\Delta_{x}\Phi)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}=−∫ℝ3∂xα∇x(τa​f−ρ)⋅∂xα(τb​f−u)​d​x+∫ℝ3∂t∂xα∇xτa​f⋅∂xατb​f​d​x+∫ℝ3∂t∂xα∇xρ⋅∂xαu​d​x\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}(\tau_{a}f-\rho)\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f-u)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\tau_{b}fdx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}udx+∫ℝ3∂xα∇xρ⋅∂xαΔx​u​d​x+∫ℝ3∂xα∇xρ⋅∂xαR2​d​x+∫ℝ3∂xα∇xτa​f⋅∂xαR3​d​x:=∑k=16Jk.\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\Delta_{x}udx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}R_{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}R_{3}dx:=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{6}J_{k}.

By a direct calculation, we haveJ1\displaystyle J_{1}≤ϵ​‖∂xαΔx​Φ‖Lx22+Cϵ​‖∂xα∇x(τb​f−u)‖Lx22,\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\Delta_{x}\Phi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\frac{C}{\epsilon}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}(\tau_{b}f-u)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2},J2\displaystyle J_{2}=−∫ℝ3∂t∂xατa​f​∂xαdivx​τb​f​d​x=‖∂xαdivx​τb​f‖Lx22,\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\tau_{a}f\partial^{\alpha}_{x}{\rm div}_{x}\tau_{b}fdx=\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}{\rm div}_{x}\tau_{b}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2},J4\displaystyle J_{4}≤ϵ​‖∂xα∇xρ‖Lx22+Cϵ​‖∂xαΔx​u‖Lx22.\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\frac{C}{\epsilon}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\Delta_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

Similarly,Jk​(k=3,5,6)J_{k}~(k=3,5,6)give the terms as followsJ3\displaystyle J_{3}=−∫ℝ3∂t∂xαρ​∂xαdivx​u​d​x=∫ℝ3∂xαdivx​u​∂xα[divx​u+divx​(ρ​u)]​d​x\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho\partial^{\alpha}_{x}{\rm div}_{x}udx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}{\rm div}_{x}u\partial^{\alpha}_{x}[{\rm div}_{x}u+{\rm div}_{x}(\rho u)]dx≤C​‖∂xα∇xu‖Lx22+C​‖u‖Hx3​(‖∇xρ‖Hx22+‖∂xα∇xu‖Lx22),\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C\|u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}(\|\nabla_{x}\rho\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}),J5\displaystyle J_{5}=∫ℝ3∂xα∇xρ⋅∂xα{−(u⋅∇x)u−ρ1+ρΔxu−[p′​(1+ρ)1+ρ−γ]∇xρ\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\left\{-(u\cdot\nabla_{x})u-\frac{\rho}{1+\rho}\Delta_{x}u-\left[\frac{p^{\prime}(1+\rho)}{1+\rho}-\gamma\right]\nabla_{x}\rho\right.−ρ1+ρ(τbf−u)−τa​f​u1+ρ}dx\displaystyle\left.\quad-\frac{\rho}{1+\rho}(\tau_{b}f-u)-\frac{\tau_{a}fu}{1+\rho}\right\}dx≤‖(τa​f,τb​f,ρ,u)‖Hx3​(‖∂xα∇xρ‖Lx22+‖(∇xρ,∇xu,Δx​u)‖Hx22),\displaystyle\leq\|(\tau_{a}f,\tau_{b}f,\rho,u)\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\left(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|(\nabla_{x}\rho,\nabla_{x}u,\Delta_{x}u)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right),J6\displaystyle J_{6}=−∫ℝ3∂xα∇xτa​f⋅∂xα(v⋅∇xP1​f,v​χ0)​d​x+∫ℝ3∂xα∇xτa​f⋅∂xα[(∇xΦ+u)​τa​f]​d​x\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}P_{1}f,v\chi_{0})dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}[(\nabla_{x}\Phi+u)\tau_{a}f]dx≤ϵ​‖∂xα∇xτa​f‖Lx22+Cϵ​‖∂xα∇xP1​f‖Lσ2​(Lx2)2+C​‖(∇xΦ,u)‖Hx3​(‖∂xα∇xτa​f‖Lx22+‖∇xτa​f‖Hx22).\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\frac{C}{\epsilon}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}P_{1}f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}(L^{2}_{x})}^{2}+C\|(\nabla_{x}\Phi,u)\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\left(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right).

Combined with (4.1), summing up the results above and choosingϵ>0\epsilon>0small enough yields (4.9).
∎

By summing(4.2)+(4.4)+A1​[A0​(4.6)+(4.9)]\eqref{geine2}+\eqref{geine3}+A_{1}[A_{0}\eqref{geine1}+\eqref{geine4}], whereA0,A1>0A_{0},A_{1}>0satisfyA0<γ2​C1A_{0}<\frac{\gamma}{2C_{1}}andA1<q0q1​(A0+1)+2A_{1}<\frac{q_{0}}{q_{1}(A_{0}+1)+2}withC1>0C_{1}>0given by (4.6), we obtaindd​t​[12​(‖f‖Lv2​(Hx3)2+γ​‖ρ‖Hx32+‖u‖Hx32+‖∇xΦ‖Hx32)+A0​A1​‖∇xΦ‖Hx22]\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{3}_{x})}^{2}+\gamma\|\rho\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right)+A_{0}A_{1}\|\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right]+dd​t​A1​∑|α|≤2[−A0​∫ℝ3∂xα∇xΦ⋅∂xα(τb​f−u)​d​x+∫ℝ3(∂xα∇xτa​f⋅∂xατb​f+∂xα∇xρ⋅∂xαu)​𝑑x]\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}A_{1}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\left[-A_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\Phi\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f-u)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\tau_{b}f+\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}u)dx\right]+[q0−q1​A1​(A0+1)−2​A1]​∑|α|≤3(‖∂xαP1​f‖Lσ2​(Lx2)2+‖∂xα∇xu‖Lx22+‖∂xα(τb​f−u)‖Lx22)\displaystyle\quad+[q_{0}-q_{1}A_{1}(A_{0}+1)-2A_{1}]\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 3}(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}P_{1}f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}(L^{2}_{x})}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f-u)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2})+A1​[(γ2−C1​A0)​‖∇xρ‖Hx22+q0​‖(∇xτa​f,Δx​Φ)‖Hx22+A0​‖∇xΦ‖Hx22]\displaystyle\quad+A_{1}\left[\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}-C_{1}A_{0}\right)\|\nabla_{x}\rho\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+q_{0}\|(\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f,\Delta_{x}\Phi)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+A_{0}\|\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right]≤C​E3​(U)​D3​(U).\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{E_{3}(U)}D_{3}(U).(4.10)

Note that∑|α|≤3(‖∂xα∇xu‖Lx22+‖∂xα(τb​f−u)‖Lx22)≥12​∑|α|≤2‖∂xα∇xτb​f‖Lx22.\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 3}(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f-u)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2})\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{b}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}.(4.11)

Therefore, there exists a constantq2>0q_{2}>0such thatdd​t​ℰ3​(U)+q2​D3​(U)≤C​E3​(U)​D3​(U),\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\mathcal{E}_{3}(U)+q_{2}D_{3}(U)\leq C\sqrt{E_{3}(U)}D_{3}(U),

whereℰ3​(U)=\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}(U)=12​(‖f‖Lv2​(Hx3)2+γ​‖ρ‖Hx32+‖u‖Hx32+‖∇xΦ‖Hx32)+A0​A1​‖∇xΦ‖Hx22\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{3}_{x})}^{2}+\gamma\|\rho\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right)+A_{0}A_{1}\|\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+∑|α|≤2A1​[−A0​∫ℝ3∂xα∇xΦ⋅∂xα(τb​f−u)​d​x+∫ℝ3(∂xα∇xτa​f⋅∂xατb​f+∂xα∇xρ⋅∂xαu)​𝑑x].\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq 2}A_{1}\left[-A_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\Phi\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f-u)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\tau_{a}f\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\tau_{b}f+\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\rho\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}u)dx\right].

By using Cauchy-Schwartz’s inequality, for sufficiently smallA0,A1>0A_{0},A_{1}>0, we have14​E3​(U)≤ℰ3​(U)≤E3​(U)\frac{1}{4}E_{3}(U)\leq\mathcal{E}_{3}(U)\leq E_{3}(U), which implies there exists a constantq3>0q_{3}>0such thatdd​t​ℰ3​(U)+q3​D3​(U)≤0.\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\mathcal{E}_{3}(U)+q_{3}D_{3}(U)\leq 0.(4.12)

Thus, we close the priori assumption (4.1). The global solution for the Cauchy problem for the VPFP/NSP system follows from the local existence Theorem5.9, the uniform energy estimate and the continuity argument.

LetHN​(U)=∑1≤|α|≤N(‖∂xαf‖Lx,v22+γ​‖∂xαρ‖Lx22+‖∂xαu‖Lx22)+∑|α|≤N‖∂xα∇xΦ‖Lx22+‖τb​f−u‖Lx22.H_{N}(U)=\sum\limits_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N}\left(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\gamma\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)+\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq N}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\tau_{b}f-u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

C\|(\tau_{a}f,\tau_{b}f,\rho,u,\nabla_{x}\Phi)\|_{H^{3}_{x}}\left(\|\nabla_{x}P_{0}f\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}(\rho,u)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+B_{1}\|\nabla_{x}\Phi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)+q_{1}\|\nabla_{x}(\tau_{a}f,\rho)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

The proof of theorem is completed.

## 4.2Optimal Time-Decay Rates of Nonlinear VPFP/NSP

Consider the following Fokker-Planck equation with damping:∂tg+v⋅∇xg−L​g+2​g=0.\partial_{t}g+v\cdot\nabla_{x}g-Lg+2g=0.(4.15)

## Lemma 4.6([35]).

then‖∫0te(t−s)​A1​Fk​(s)​𝑑s‖Lv,ς+1∞​(Lx2)\displaystyle\left\|\int^{t}_{0}e^{(t-s)A_{1}}F_{k}(s)ds\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{v,\varsigma+1}(L^{2}_{x})}≤C​(1+t)−c0,\displaystyle\leq C(1+t)^{-c_{0}},(4.18)‖∫0t∇ve(t−s)​A1​Fk​(s)​𝑑s‖Lv,ς∞​(Lx2)\displaystyle\left\|\int^{t}_{0}\nabla_{v}e^{(t-s)A_{1}}F_{k}(s)ds\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{v,\varsigma}(L^{2}_{x})}≤C​(1+t)−c0,\displaystyle\leq C(1+t)^{-c_{0}},(4.19)‖∫0te(t−s)​A1​F2​(s)​𝑑s‖Lv,0∞​(Lx2)\displaystyle\left\|\int^{t}_{0}e^{(t-s)A_{1}}F_{2}(s)ds\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{v,0}(L^{2}_{x})}≤C​(1+t)−c0.\displaystyle\leq C(1+t)^{-c_{0}}.(4.20)

For instance, by (4.21) and Theorem3.6, we obtain‖U​(t)‖Z2\displaystyle\|U(t)\|_{Z^{2}}≥‖et​𝕄​U0‖Z2−∫0t‖e(t−s)​𝕄​R​(s)‖Z2​𝑑s≥C1​d0​(1+t)−34−C2​δ02​(1+t)−34.\displaystyle\geq\|e^{t\mathbb{M}}U_{0}\|_{Z^{2}}-\int^{t}_{0}\|e^{(t-s)\mathbbm{M}}R(s)\|_{Z^{2}}ds\geq C_{1}d_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}-C_{2}\delta_{0}^{2}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}.

infinitesimal generator of aC0C_{0}-semigroup onHH.

For thennlinearly independent eigenvectorsVi∈HV_{i}\in HofAA, we have0=(Vi,A∗​Vj¯−λj¯⋅Vj¯)H=(A​Vi,Vj¯)H−λj​(Vi,Vj¯)H=(λi−λj)​(Vi,Vj¯)H,0=(V_{i},A^{*}\overline{V_{j}}-\overline{\lambda_{j}}\cdot\overline{V_{j}})_{H}=(AV_{i},\overline{V_{j}})_{H}-\lambda_{j}(V_{i},\overline{V_{j}})_{H}=(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})(V_{i},\overline{V_{j}})_{H},

withn≥0n\geq 0and the initial dataUn+1​(0,x,v)=U0=(f0​(x,v),ρ0​(x),u0​(x)).U^{n+1}(0,x,v)=U_{0}=(f_{0}(x,v),\rho_{0}(x),u_{0}(x)).

## Proof.

First, we claim thatUn∈X3​(0,T∗;M0)U^{n}\in X_{3}(0,T^{*};M_{0}). By taking the inner product betweenχ0\chi_{0}and(5.11)1\eqref{locale1}_{1}, we have∂tτa​fn+1+divx​τb​fn+1=0,\partial_{t}\tau_{a}f^{n+1}+{\rm div}_{x}\tau_{b}f^{n+1}=0,

and then−∫ℝ3∂xα∇xΦn+1⋅∂xα(τb​fn+1−un+1)​d​x=∫ℝ3∂xαΦn+1​∂xαdivx​(τb​fn+1−un+1)​d​x\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n+1}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f^{n+1}-u^{n+1})dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\Phi^{n+1}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}{\rm div}_{x}(\tau_{b}f^{n+1}-u^{n+1})dx=12​dd​t​‖∂xα∇xΦn+1‖Lx22+∫ℝ3∂xαΦn+1​∂xα(un⋅∇xρn+1+ρn​divx​un+1)​d​x.\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\Phi^{n+1}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(u^{n}\cdot\nabla_{x}\rho^{n+1}+\rho^{n}{\rm div}_{x}u^{n+1})dx.(5.13)

Forn=0n=0, applying∂xα\partial^{\alpha}_{x}with0≤|α|≤N0\leq|\alpha|\leq Nto(5.11)1\eqref{locale1}_{1},(5.11)2\eqref{locale1}_{2},(5.11)3\eqref{locale1}_{3}, respectively, and then multiplying them by∂xαf1,∂xαρ1\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{1},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho^{1}and∂xαu1\partial^{\alpha}_{x}u^{1}, respectively, taking integration and summation, using (5.2), we obtain12​dd​t​(‖∂xαf1‖Lx,v22+‖∂xα(ρ1,u1,∇xΦ1)‖Lx22)+∫ℝ3(−L​∂xαf1,∂xαf1)​𝑑x+‖∂xα∇xu1‖Lx22\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\left(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\rho^{1},u^{1},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{1})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(-L\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{1},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{1})dx+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}u^{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}=−∫ℝ3∂xαρ1​∂xαdivx​u1​d​x−∫ℝ3∂xα∇xΦ1⋅∂xα(τb​f1−u1)​d​x\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho^{1}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}{\rm div}_{x}u^{1}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\Phi^{1}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f^{1}-u^{1})dx≤12​‖∂xα∇xu1‖Lx22+12​‖∂xαρ1‖Lx22+‖∂xα(τb​f1,u1,∇xΦ1)‖Lx22.\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}u^{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho^{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f^{1},u^{1},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.(5.14)

Summing up the result above over|α|≤3|\alpha|\leq 3,dd​t​E3​(U1​(t))+μ0​D3​(U1​(t))≤C​E3​(U1​(t)),\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t))+\mu_{0}\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{1}(t))\leq C\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t)),

whereDN​(U)=‖Pr​f‖Lσ2​(HxN)2+‖∇xu‖HxN2.\mathrm{D}_{N}(U)=\|P_{r}f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}(H^{N}_{x})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}u\|_{H^{N}_{x}}^{2}.

Integrating the result above over[0,T∗][0,T^{*}]and taking the supremum in time, we havesup0≤t≤T∗E3​(U1​(t))+μ0​∫0T∗D3​(U1​(t))​𝑑t≤M0+C​T∗​sup0≤t≤T∗E3​(U1​(t)).\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t))+\mu_{0}\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{1}(t))dt\leq M_{0}+CT^{*}\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t)).

Thus, we chooseT∗>0T^{*}>0sufficiently small such thatC​T∗<1/2CT^{*}<1/2, and hence,sup0≤t≤T∗E3​(U1​(t))≤M01−C​T∗≤2​M0.\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t))\leq\frac{M_{0}}{1-CT^{*}}\leq 2M_{0}.

Moreover, it holds that∫0T∗D3​(U1​(t))​𝑑t≤C​M0\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{1}(t))dt\leq CM_{0}. On the other hand, combined with(L​f,f)=−‖∇vPr​f‖2−14​‖v​Pr​f‖2+32​‖Pr​f‖2≥−‖Pr​f‖Lσ22,\displaystyle(Lf,f)=-\|\nabla_{v}P_{r}f\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\|vP_{r}f\|^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\|P_{r}f\|^{2}\geq-\|P_{r}f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2},

we have by (5.2)12​dd​t​(‖∂xαf1‖Lx,v22+‖∂xα(ρ1,u1,∇xΦ1)‖Lx22)\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\left(\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\rho^{1},u^{1},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{1})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)≥−‖Pr​f‖Lσ22−32​‖∂xα∇xu1‖Lx22−12​‖∂xαρ1‖Lx22−‖∂xα(τb​f1,u1,∇xΦ1)‖Lx22,\displaystyle\geq-\|P_{r}f\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}}^{2}-\frac{3}{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}u^{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho^{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}-\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\tau_{b}f^{1},u^{1},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2},

and thendd​t​E3​(U1​(t))≥−C​[D3​(U1​(t))+E3​(U1​(t))].\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t))\geq-C[\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{1}(t))+\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t))].

Thus, the continuity ofE3​(U1​(t))\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t))follows from|E3(U1(t))−E3(U1(s))|=|∫stdd​τE3(U1(τ)dτ|≤C∫stD3(U1(τ))dτ+CM0|t−s|→0,t→s.\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(t))-\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(s))\right|=\left|\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}\tau}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{1}(\tau)d\tau\right|\leq C\int_{s}^{t}D_{3}(U^{1}(\tau))d\tau+CM_{0}|t-s|\to 0,~t\to s.(5.15)

Combining the results above, we obtainU1∈X3​(0,T∗;M0)U^{1}\in X_{3}(0,T^{*};M_{0}).

Suppose thatUk∈X3​(0,T∗;M0)U^{k}\in X_{3}(0,T^{*};M_{0})and∫0T∗D3​(Uk​(t))​𝑑t≤C​M0\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{k}(t))dt\leq CM_{0}fork≤nk\leq n. Fork=n+1k=n+1, similar to the treatment of (4.4), applying∂xα\partial^{\alpha}_{x}with0≤|α|≤N0\leq|\alpha|\leq Nto(5.11)1\eqref{locale1}_{1},(5.11)2\eqref{locale1}_{2},(5.11)3\eqref{locale1}_{3}, respectively, and then multiplying them by∂xαfn+1,∂xαρn+1\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{n+1},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\rho^{n+1}and∂xαun+1\partial^{\alpha}_{x}u^{n+1}, respectively, making use of (5.2), taking integration and summation, we obtain12​dd​t​(‖fn+1‖Lv2​(Hx3)2+‖(ρn+1,un+1,∇xΦn+1)‖Hx32)+μ0​(‖Pr​fn+1‖Lσ2​(Hx3)2+‖∇xun+1‖Hx32)\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\left(\|f^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{3}_{x})}^{2}+\|(\rho^{n+1},u^{n+1},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n+1})\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right)+\mu_{0}\left(\|P_{r}f^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}(H^{3}_{x})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}u^{n+1}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right)≤C​(1+‖(ρn,un,∇xΦn)‖Hx32)​(‖fn+1‖Lv2​(Hx3)2+‖(ρn+1,un+1,∇xΦn+1)‖Hx32)\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\|(\rho^{n},u^{n},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n})\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right)\left(\|f^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{3}_{x})}^{2}+\|(\rho^{n+1},u^{n+1},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n+1})\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right)+C​‖(ρn,un,∇xΦn)‖Hx32​(1+‖τa​fn‖Hx32)+C​‖(τa​fn,τb​fn,un)‖Hx32\displaystyle\quad+C\|(\rho^{n},u^{n},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n})\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}(1+\|\tau_{a}f^{n}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2})+C\|(\tau_{a}f^{n},\tau_{b}f^{n},u^{n})\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}+C​‖(ρn,un,∇xΦn)‖Hx32​(‖Pr​fn‖Lσ2​(Hx3)2+‖∇xun‖Hx32),\displaystyle\quad+C\|(\rho^{n},u^{n},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n})\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\left(\|P_{r}f^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}(H^{3}_{x})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}u^{n}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right),

and hence12​dd​t​E3​(Un+1​(t))+μ0​D3​(Un+1​(t))\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))+\mu_{0}\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))≤C​[1+E3​(Un​(t))]​E3​(Un+1​(t))+C​E3​(Un​(t))​[1+C​E3​(Un​(t))+D3​(Un​(t))].\displaystyle\leq C[1+\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n}(t))]\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))+C\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n}(t))[1+C\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n}(t))+\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{n}(t))].(5.16)

Integrating (5.16) over[0,T∗][0,T^{*}]and taking the supremum in time yields[1−C​T∗​(1+M0)]​sup0≤t≤T∗E3​(Un+1​(t))≤M0+C​T∗​(M0+M02)+C​M02.[1-CT^{*}(1+M_{0})]\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))\leq M_{0}+CT^{*}(M_{0}+M_{0}^{2})+CM_{0}^{2}.(5.17)

By choosingT∗>0T^{*}>0sufficiently small, we obtainsup0≤t≤T∗E3​(Un+1​(t))≤2​M0.\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))\leq 2M_{0}.

Moreover, it holds that∫0T∗D3​(Un+1​(t))​𝑑t≤C​M0\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))dt\leq CM_{0}. Similar to the treatment of (5.15), the continuity ofE3​(Un+1​(t))\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))follows from|E3(Un+1(t))−E3(Un+1(s))|=|∫stdd​τE3(Un+1(τ)dτ|\displaystyle|\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))-\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n+1}(s))|=\left|\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}\tau}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n+1}(\tau)d\tau\right|≤C​M0​∫st[D3​(Un​(t))+D3​(Un+1​(t))]​𝑑τ+C​(M0+M02)​|t−s|→0,t→s.\displaystyle\leq CM_{0}\int_{s}^{t}[\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{n}(t))+\mathrm{D}_{3}(U^{n+1}(t))]d\tau+C(M_{0}+M_{0}^{2})|t-s|\to 0,~t\to s.(5.18)

Combining the results above, we obtainUn+1​(t)∈X3​(0,T∗;M0)U^{n+1}(t)\in X_{3}(0,T^{*};M_{0})withn∈ℕn\in\mathbb{N}. This completes the induction.

DenoteU~1=U1,U~n+1=(f~n+1,ρ~n+1,u~n+1):=(fn+1−fn,ρn+1−ρn,un+1−un),n≥1,\tilde{U}^{1}=U^{1},~~\tilde{U}^{n+1}=(\tilde{f}^{n+1},\tilde{\rho}^{n+1},\tilde{u}^{n+1}):=(f^{n+1}-f^{n},\rho^{n+1}-\rho^{n},u^{n+1}-u^{n}),~~n\geq 1,

and study the following system ofU~n+1​(n≥1)\tilde{U}^{n+1}~(n\geq 1)∂tf~n+1+v⋅∇xf~n+1−L​f~n+1\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{f}^{n+1}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{f}^{n+1}-L\tilde{f}^{n+1}=(∇xΦn−1+un−1)⋅(12​v​f~n−∇vf~n)+(∇xΦ~n+u~n)⋅(12​v​fn−∇vfn+v​χ0),\displaystyle\quad=(\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n-1}+u^{n-1})\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}v\tilde{f}^{n}-\nabla_{v}\tilde{f}^{n}\right)+(\nabla_{x}\tilde{\Phi}^{n}+\tilde{u}^{n})\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}vf^{n}-\nabla_{v}f^{n}+v\chi_{0}\right),∂tρ~n+1+divx​u~n+1+un⋅∇xρ~n+1+u~n⋅∇xρn+ρn​divx​u~n+1+ρ~n​divx​un=0,\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{\rho}^{n+1}+{\rm div}_{x}\tilde{u}^{n+1}+u^{n}\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{\rho}^{n+1}+\tilde{u}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{x}\rho^{n}+\rho^{n}{\rm div}_{x}\tilde{u}^{n+1}+\tilde{\rho}^{n}{\rm div}_{x}u^{n}=0,∂tu~n+1−11+ρn​Δx​u~n+1\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{u}^{n+1}-\frac{1}{1+\rho^{n}}\Delta_{x}\tilde{u}^{n+1}=−u~n⋅∇xun−un−1⋅∇xu~n−∇xΦ~n−[p′​(1+ρn)1+ρn−p′​(1+ρn−1)1+ρn−1]​∇xρn\displaystyle\quad=-\tilde{u}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{n}-u^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{u}^{n}-\nabla_{x}\tilde{\Phi}^{n}-\left[\frac{p^{\prime}(1+\rho^{n})}{1+\rho^{n}}-\frac{p^{\prime}(1+\rho^{n-1})}{1+\rho^{n-1}}\right]\nabla_{x}\rho^{n}−p′​(1+ρn−1)1+ρn−1​∇xρ~n+(11+ρn−11+ρn−1)​(Δx​un+τb​fn−un−τa​fn​un)\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{p^{\prime}(1+\rho^{n-1})}{1+\rho^{n-1}}\nabla_{x}\tilde{\rho}^{n}+\left(\frac{1}{1+\rho^{n}}-\frac{1}{1+\rho^{n-1}}\right)(\Delta_{x}u^{n}+\tau_{b}f^{n}-u^{n}-\tau_{a}f^{n}u^{n})+11+ρn−1​(τb​f~n−u~n−τa​f~n​un−τa​fn−1​u~n)\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{1}{1+\rho^{n-1}}(\tau_{b}\tilde{f}^{n}-\tilde{u}^{n}-\tau_{a}\tilde{f}^{n}u^{n}-\tau_{a}f^{n-1}\tilde{u}^{n})Δx​Φ~n+1=τa​f~n+1−ρ~n+1,\displaystyle\Delta_{x}\tilde{\Phi}^{n+1}=\tau_{a}\tilde{f}^{n+1}-\tilde{\rho}^{n+1},

with the initial dataU~1​(0,x,v)=U0,U~n+1​(0,x,v)=0,n≥1.\tilde{U}^{1}(0,x,v)=U_{0},~~\tilde{U}^{n+1}(0,x,v)=0,~~n\geq 1.

SinceUnU^{n}only hasHx3H^{3}_{x}regularity, we can only establish anHx2H^{2}_{x}estimate forU~n+1\tilde{U}^{n+1}due to the terms such asu~n⋅∇xρn\tilde{u}^{n}\cdot\nabla_{x}\rho^{n}. With this, we now show thatUnU^{n}is a Cauchy sequence inX2​(0,T∗;M0)X_{2}(0,T^{*};M_{0}). It is easy to verify thatU~1​(t)∈X2​(0,T∗;M0)\tilde{U}^{1}(t)\in X_{2}(0,T^{*};M_{0}). Similar to the process of (5.16), we obtain12​dd​t​(‖f~n+1‖Lv2​(Hx2)2+‖(ρ~n+1,u~n+1,∇xΦ~n+1)‖Hx22)+μ0​(‖Pr​f~n+1‖Lσ2​(Hx2)2+‖∇xu~n+1‖Hx22)\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\left(\|\tilde{f}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}+\|(\tilde{\rho}^{n+1},\tilde{u}^{n+1},\nabla_{x}\tilde{\Phi}^{n+1})\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)+\mu_{0}\left(\|P_{r}\tilde{f}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{u}^{n+1}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)≤C​(1+‖(ρn,un)‖Hx32)​(‖f~n+1‖Lv2​(Hx2)2+‖(ρ~n+1,u~n+1,∇xΦ~n+1)‖Hx22)\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\|(\rho^{n},u^{n})\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}\right)\left(\|\tilde{f}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}+\|(\tilde{\rho}^{n+1},\tilde{u}^{n+1},\nabla_{x}\tilde{\Phi}^{n+1})\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)+C​(1+‖(ρn−1,un−1,∇xΦn−1)‖Hx22)​‖f~n‖Lv2​(Hx2)2\displaystyle\quad+C\left(1+\|(\rho^{n-1},u^{n-1},\nabla_{x}\Phi^{n-1})\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)\|\tilde{f}^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}+C​(1+‖ρn−1‖Hx32+‖fn‖Lv2​(Hx3)2)​‖ρ~n‖Hx22\displaystyle\quad+C\left(1+\|\rho^{n-1}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}+\|f^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{3}_{x})}^{2}\right)\|\tilde{\rho}^{n}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C​(1+‖(fn−1,fn)‖Lv2​(Hx3)2+‖ρn−1‖Hx32+‖fn‖Lv2​(Hx2)2​‖ρn‖Hx22)​‖u~n‖Hx22\displaystyle\quad+C\left(1+\|(f^{n-1},f^{n})\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{3}_{x})}^{2}+\|\rho^{n-1}\|_{H^{3}_{x}}^{2}+\|f^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}\|\rho^{n}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right)\|\tilde{u}^{n}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C​(1+‖fn‖Lv2​(Hx2)2)​‖∇xΦ~n‖Hx22.\displaystyle\quad+C\left(1+\|f^{n}\|_{L^{2}_{v}(H^{2}_{x})}^{2}\right)\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{\Phi}^{n}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

Similar to the treatment of (5.17), we have[1−C​T∗​(1+M0)]​sup0≤t≤T∗E2​(U~n+1​(t))≤C​T∗​(1+M0+M02)​sup0≤t≤T∗E2​(U~n​(t)).[1-CT^{*}(1+M_{0})]\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{2}(\tilde{U}^{n+1}(t))\leq CT^{*}(1+M_{0}+M_{0}^{2})\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{2}(\tilde{U}^{n}(t)).

By choosingT∗>0T^{*}>0sufficiently small, we obtain forκ~1∈(0,1)\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\in(0,1)thatsup0≤t≤T∗E2​(U~n+1​(t))≤κ~1​sup0≤t≤T∗E2​(U~n​(t)),\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{2}(\tilde{U}^{n+1}(t))\leq\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{2}(\tilde{U}^{n}(t)),(5.19)

which implies thatUn=(fn,ρn,un)U^{n}=(f^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})is a Cauchy sequence in the Banach spaceX2​(0,T∗;M0)X_{2}(0,T^{*};M_{0}). Hence, there exists a limit functionU=(f,ρ,u)∈X2​(0,T∗;M0)U=(f,\rho,u)\in X_{2}(0,T^{*};M_{0})such thatUn→UU^{n}\to Ustrongly inX2​(0,T∗;M0)X_{2}(0,T^{*};M_{0})asn→∞n\to\infty, which solves the Cauchy problem (1.2). Moreover, the uniform boundsup0≤t≤T∗E3​(Un​(t))≤2​M0\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n}(t))\leq 2M_{0}admits a weakly convergent subsequence still denoted byUn=(fn,ρn,un)U^{n}=(f^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})such thatUn⇀UU^{n}\rightharpoonup Uasn→∞n\to\infty. Combined with the weak lower semicontinuity of the Sobolev norm, we obtainsup0≤t≤T∗E3​(U​(t))≤lim infn→∞sup0≤t≤T∗E3​(Un​(t))≤2​M0.\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U(t))\leq\liminf\limits_{n\to\infty}\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{3}(U^{n}(t))\leq 2M_{0}.

The proof of the continuity ofE3​(U​(t))\mathrm{E}_{3}(U(t))is similar to (5.18). Thus, we haveU=(f,ρ,u)∈X3​(0,T∗;M0)U=(f,\rho,u)\in X_{3}(0,T^{*};M_{0}). For the uniqueness, letU¯=(f¯,ρ¯,u¯)∈X3​(0,T∗;M0)\overline{U}=(\overline{f},\overline{\rho},\overline{u})\in X_{3}(0,T^{*};M_{0})be another solution. Similar to the process of (5.19), we have forκ~2∈(0,1)\tilde{\kappa}_{2}\in(0,1)thatsup0≤t≤T∗E2​(U​(t)−U¯​(t))≤κ~2​sup0≤t≤T∗E2​(U​(t)−U¯​(t)),\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{2}(U(t)-\overline{U}(t))\leq\tilde{\kappa}_{2}\sup\limits_{0\leq t\leq T^{*}}\mathrm{E}_{2}(U(t)-\overline{U}(t)),

which impliesU=U¯U=\overline{U}. The proof of theorem is completed.
